Dengue Vaccine in India, First Shot Expected in 2027 - Times Bull

News Brief
The article from Times Bull, published on November 4, 2026, discusses the upcoming launch of the dengue vaccine Qdenga in India in 2027. The vaccine, approved by the Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation (CDSCO) in July 2026, is designed to target all four serotypes of the dengue virus (DENV-1 to DENV-4). It is developed using a weakened DENV-2 backbone with surface proteins from other serotypes. While the vaccine has shown efficacy of 61.2% against confirmed dengue and 84.1% against hospitalisation 4.5 years post-vaccination, it does not prevent all infections. The World Health Organization (WHO) recommends its use for children aged six to 16 in high-transmission areas but highlights uncertainty about protection against DENV-3 and DENV-4 in those without prior infection. Clinicians, including Dr Sanjeev Bagai, have raised concerns about the vaccine’s effectiveness and potential risks, such as antibody-dependent enhancement (ADE), and call for further evaluation. The article also notes that dengue cases in India have increased significantly over the years, with reported cases dropping by November 2025 but likely underreported due to the disease's year-round nature and the possibility of coinfections involving multiple serotypes.
Original source text (English)
Skip to content Times Bull Breaking News, Latest Headlines & India News Updates Home Photos Web Stories Live Blog For You Whatsapp search Live Blogs Whatsapp Business Gadgets Auto Astrology India Entertainment Sports Hindi News Health jobs Education lifestyle Recipes State World Delhi Uttar Pradesh Madhya Pradesh Rajasthan Bihar Haryana Chhattisgarh Himachal Pradesh Jammu & Kashmir Maharashtra Jharkhand Business Gadgets Auto Hindi News India Sports Astrology Education Entertainment Health jobs Recipes lifestyle World State State Delhi Uttar Pradesh Madhya Pradesh Rajasthan Bihar Haryana Chhattisgarh Himachal Pradesh Jammu & Kashmir Maharashtra Jharkhand Search Search for: Home Business Dengue Vaccine in India, First Shot Expected in 2027 Dengue Vaccine in India, First Shot Expected in 2027 Infection with one serotype does not confer immunity against the others, meaning individuals can be infected multiple times. An initial infection may be mild or unnoticed, but subsequent infection with a different serotype increases the risk of severe illness in some cases. Sweta Mitra November 04, 2026 - 11:46 AM Join Follow Follow Dengue Vaccine: The Central Drugs Standard Control Organisation (CDSCO) approved Qdenga in July for individuals aged four to 60. Takeda plans to launch the vaccine in India in partnership with Dr Reddy’s Laboratories. Until now, there has been no widely accessible vaccine to protect against dengue, which is the fastest-growing mosquito-borne disease globally. According to a World Health Organisation surveillance system, 14.1 million cases were recorded worldwide in 2024, the highest number ever documented, with cases reported in over 100 countries. India is included in this global situation. Dengue Vaccine Update In India, reported dengue cases increased from 44,585 in 2020 to 193,245 in 2021, with 233,519 cases and 297 deaths recorded in 2024. By November 2025, cases had dropped to 113,440 with 94 deaths, the lowest figures since 2021, though official statistics underestimate the true scale of the problem. One Indian study estimated that actual cases could be 282 times higher than reported, and dengue has become a year-round issue rather than one confined to certain seasons. Against this backdrop, the introduction of a vaccine offers hope. However, some clinicians argue that the evidence supporting the vaccine requires more thorough evaluation before it is widely adopted. Prominent paediatrician Dr Sanjeev Bagai has expressed concerns, including the virus’s evolution, regional variations in circulating strains across India, and antibody-dependent enhancement (ADE), where the immune response from a prior infection may worsen a subsequent one. Dengue is primarily transmitted through the bite of infected Aedes aegypti mosquitoes and has four serotypes, DENV-1 to DENV-4. Infection with one serotype does not confer immunity against the others, meaning individuals can be infected multiple times. An initial infection may be mild or unnoticed, but subsequent infection with a different serotype increases the risk of severe illness in some cases. This presents a major challenge for vaccine development. India’s dengue burden is further complicated by several serotypes circulating in different regions. Dr Bagai notes that dominant strains vary geographically and shift over time. “It is impossible to predict which strain will dominate in which region of India at any given time of year,” he explains. He also highlights coinfections involving multiple serotypes and genetic changes in the virus, which complicate assessments of a vaccine’s nationwide effectiveness. Qdenga is a tetravalent vaccine designed to target all four serotypes, but it is developed using a weakened DENV-2 backbone into which the genes for the surface proteins of the other serotypes are inserted. Dr Bagai questions whether this approach ensures balanced protection given the variability in circulating strains. Nonetheless, the vaccine does include components for all four serotypes and is not solely targeted at DENV-2. Dr Bagai’s initial concern relates to the duration of protection. Trial results are promising: Qdenga has been shown to protect against virologically confirmed dengue and reduce dengue-related hospitalisations, although efficacy varies by serotype and prior exposure. A long-term analysis of the pivotal trial, published by the manufacturer, reported an efficacy of 61.2% against confirmed dengue and 84.1% against hospitalisation 4.5 years post-vaccination. While this represents significant protection, it does not prevent all infections. Dr Bagai calls for closer examination of how protection evolves over subsequent years, particularly for individual serotypes. What is the issue? “The issue is that people tend to focus on headline vaccine efficacy numbers without considering how protection changes over three or four years,” he says. He also questions whether trial data sufficiently represent India’s diverse population and dengue’s geographical distribution. However, waning protection does not indicate harm. A reduction in efficacy alone does not demonstrate that later infections become more severe. The World Health Organization (WHO) recommends Qdenga for children aged six to 16 in areas with high transmission but notes uncertainty about protection against DENV-3 and DENV-4 in individuals without prior infection. It advises against routine use in low-to-moderate transmission regions until further evidence is available and urges countries to consider regional transmission differences rather than applying a uniform policy. THE IMMUNITY CONCERN Dr Bagai’s most serious worry is ADE. Antibodies generated from an earlier infection may fail to neutralise a subsequent infection and can facilitate the virus’s entry into cells, increasing the risk of severe disease. Dengue is a well-known example: a second infection with a different serotype can provoke a stronger inflammatory reaction and complications. Given the wide variation in individuals’ exposure histories, Dr Bagai emphasises the need to evaluate vaccinations in this context. “If you are administering a vaccine, you must understand how the individual’s existing immune status will respond,” he states. He also suggests that prior infections such as COVID-19 and influenza might influence responses to dengue vaccination due to similarities in immune-recognition pathways. However, assertions that shared protein sequences might cause the immune system to react as if facing another dengue infection are not yet established clinical evidence of ADE following Qdenga vaccination. Such claims require specific validation. Loading... Home Photo Gallery Web Stories For You

佛罗里达州登革热病例激增至 485 例,美国数十年来最大的 暴发 - 萨拉索塔先驱论坛报

Florida dengue cases surge to 485 in largest outbreak in US in decades - Sarasota Herald-Tribune
新闻简报据佛罗里达州卫生部称,佛罗里达州正在经历数十年来最大规模的登革热 暴发,截至 2026 年 10 月 24 日,已有 485 例确诊病例。其中大部分病例(385 例)集中在西海岸的希尔斯伯勒县。暴发 已蔓延到 12 个县,包括皮内拉斯、迈阿密戴德县和奥兰治。希尔斯伯勒县确认一例死亡,原因是登革热感染、感染性休克和多器官衰竭。该州已将当地紧急状态声明延长至 2026 年 11 月 5 日,以解决蚊子数量问题并防止进一步的 传播。灭蚊人员正在加强治疗,并敦促居民清除其房产上的积水。佛罗里达州卫生部正在与当地合作伙伴合作,监测和应对 暴发。登革热也称为断骨热,会引起剧烈疼痛,在某些情况下可能危及生命。虽然 暴发 很重要,但西尼罗河病毒和基孔肯雅热等其他蚊子传播疾病的报告数量较少。国家正在推广非化学害虫防治方法来控制这种情况。

时间
暴发 的报告截至 2026 年 10 月 24 日,最新更新提到一周内新增 60 例本地感染病例(N24、N6)。发布日期为2026年10月30日(N3)。
地点与对象
暴发 主要位于佛罗里达州,特别是西海岸的希尔斯伯勒县,该县有 385 例确诊病例。其他受影响的县包括皮内拉斯县、迈阿密戴德县、奥兰治县、帕斯科县、柑橘县、海牛县、布劳沃德县、棕榈滩县、波尔克县等。全州总数为 485 例(N24、N12)。

共有 485 例 登革热 病例,其中 385 例位于希尔斯伯勒县。希尔斯伯勒县确认了 1 例死亡,原因是登革热感染、感染性休克和多器官衰竭 (N15)。暴发已蔓延至12个县,还有6例西尼罗河病毒阳性样本和1例基孔肯雅热(N25)病例。

Florida dengue cases surge to 485 in largest outbreak in US in decades - Sarasota Herald-Tribune

News Brief
Florida is experiencing its largest dengue outbreak in decades, with 485 confirmed cases as of October 24, 2026, according to the Florida Department of Health. The majority of these cases, 385, are concentrated in Hillsborough County on the west coast. The outbreak has spread to 12 counties, including Pinellas, Miami-Dade, and Orange. One death was confirmed in Hillsborough County, attributed to dengue infection, septic shock, and multi-organ failure. The state has extended a local emergency declaration until November 5, 2026, to address the mosquito population and prevent further transmission. Mosquito management crews are intensifying treatments, and residents are urged to eliminate standing water on their property. The Florida Department of Health is working with local partners to monitor and respond to the outbreak. Dengue, also known as breakbone fever, causes severe aches and pains and can be life-threatening in some cases. While the outbreak is significant, other mosquito-transmitted diseases like West Nile virus and chikungunya have been reported in smaller numbers. The state is promoting non-chemical pest control methods to manage the situation.
Original source text (English)
News Sports Ticket Lifestyle Real Estate Advertise Obituaries eNewspaper Legals STATE Florida dengue cases surge to 485 in largest outbreak in US in decades Cheryl McCloud C. A. Bridges USA TODAY NETWORK - Florida Oct. 30, 2026, 10:18 a.m. ET Here are symptoms of dengue fever you should watch for. How can you reduce the chances of getting mosquito-transmitted diseases in Florida? Florida reported 60 more locally acquired cases of dengue fever in one week, according to the latest update from the Florida Department of Health. The disease is also called breakbone fever, due to the pain it causes. That brings the statewide total to 485 for the year as of Oct. 24, the largest outbreak in the United States in decades. The previous highest number of Florida cases in recent memory was 199 in 2023. In 2025, Florida reported 62 cases for the year. ➤ Prevent dengue at home in Florida with this 10-minute mosquito inspection The bulk of the cases continues to be in Hillsborough County on Florida's west coast, which has 385 confirmed cases. Locally acquired cases have now been reported in 12 counties. The outbreak prompted Hillsborough County Administrator Bonnie Wise to extend a state of local emergency to Nov. 5 in the county as officials "work to control the mosquito population and protect residents from dengue fever." One death was confirmed in Hillsborough County, identified as 80-year-old Gay Small, who died of “dengue infection” along with septic shock and multi-organ failure, according to a death certificate. The previous largest outbreak was in Hawaii's Big Island, where 264 local cases were reported in the six months between September 2015 and March 2016. President Trump in September issued an order for a "campaign" to reduce the mosquito and tick population in Washington, D.C. That model could then be "applied as targeted, innovative, non-chemical pest control for the whole nation." Dengue isn't the only mosquito-transmitted disease that has popped up this year in Florida, although those have been in far fewer numbers than dengue: West Nile Virus, Chikungunya and Eastern Equine Encephalitis. Another case of West Nile Virus was reported in Sarasota County, bringing the 2026 total to seven cases, although two were asymptomatic. Here's what you should know about mosquito-borne illnesses in Florida, symptoms to watch for, and how best to prevent mosquito bites, including advice from the Florida Department of Health. 18 counties under mosquito-borne illness advisories, alerts A mosquito-borne illness advisory has been issued for the following counties: Bay Brevard Broward Holmes Leon Manatee Martin Palm Beach Polk Walton A mosquito-borne illness alert has been issued for the following counties: Alachua Citrus Duval Hillsborough Miami-Dade Orange Pasco Pinellas "A mosquito-borne illness advisory means the public should take precautions to reduce mosquito bites due to factors indicating local disease such as positive sentinels, very few presumptive or positive human cases, or other justifications," according to Sarasota County. "A mosquito-borne illness alert means that there has been heightened disease activity, a significant increase in vector (disease capable) mosquito populations, or an increase in locally-transmitted human cases. "Residents should continue to take precautions, but exercise additional caution in preventing mosquito bites." Dengue cases increasing in Florida, with more counties reporting mosquito-transmitted disease According to the Florida Department of Health, as of Oct. 24, there have been 485 cases of locally acquired cases of dengue reported in 12 counties, up from 425 cases reported Oct. 17. There have also been six positive samples of locally acquired of West Nile virus and one case of chikungunya. Dengue: 485 cases 385 in Hillsborough County 67 in Pinellas County 9 in Miami-Dade County 9 in Orange County 4 in Pasco County 3 in Citrus County 2 in Manatee County 1 in Brevard County 1 in Broward County 1 in Palm Beach County 1 in Polk County West Nile: seven cases 2 in Alachua County 1 in Duval County (asymptomatic blood donors) 1 in Holmes County 1 in Leon (asymptomatic blood donor) 1 in Sarasota County 1 in Walton County Chikungunya: 1 case 1 in Miami-Dade County Eastern Equine Encephalitis: 1 case 1 in Leon County What is Hillsborough County doing to try to control mosquitoes? A local state of emergency has been extended in Hillsborough County until Nov. 5. A state of local emergency can be in effect for only seven days, unless rescinded or extended, according to the Hillsborough County Department of Health. "Mosquito management crews are increasing treatments by air and ground because the county’s mosquito surveillance in many neighborhoods is showing dengue virus activity in the mosquito population. "Residents are encouraged to look for and eliminate standing water on their property after recent heavy rains." Residents can sign up to be notified where aerial treatment will occur in neighborhoods. You can also go to the county's website to see when and where mosquito "operations" are scheduled to take place. Trump wants mosquitoes, ticks eliminated in Washington, D.C. On Sept. 29, Trump signed an executive order to eliminate ticks and mosquitoes in Washington, D.C. The order directs the Secretary of the Interior, the Secretary of Agriculture, the Administrator of the Environmental Protection Agency, and the Administrator of General Services, along with the Secretary of Health and Human Services, to "develop and implement a campaign across Washington, D.C., to reduce invasive mosquito populations by 90 percent and the tick population by 50 percent by 2028 using safe and effective, non-chemical, and innovative technology-enabled methods. "This new model, localized to Washington, D.C., can later be applied nationwide as targeted, innovative, non-chemical pest control for the whole nation." Florida residents should take precautions to avoid mosquito-borne illnesses "Residents throughout the state should continue to take precautions from mosquito-borne illnesses by appropriately applying bug spray, avoiding areas with high mosquito populations, and wearing long pants and shirts when possible — especially during sunrise and sunset when mosquitos are most active," said Isabella White, FDOH press secretary via email Sept. 23. White said FDOH "continues to coordinate with local mosquito control partners to support surveillance and targeted response efforts in affected areas. These efforts help reduce mosquito populations, limit the risk of disease transmission, and protect residents across Florida." What are symptoms of dengue, breakbone fever? "Dengue fever can be a painful, debilitating disease but is rarely fatal," the Florida Department of Health said. Symptoms appear three to 14 days after the bite of an infected mosquito and include: sudden onset of fever severe headache eye pain muscle and joint pain (giving the disease the nickname “breakbone fever”) bleeding Gastrointestinal symptoms like vomiting and diarrhea may also be present in some cases. "Dengue fever symptoms usually last four to seven days. The disease is often diagnosed incorrectly because the symptoms are similar to influenza and other viruses," the CDC said. "Around one out of every 20 people infected by a dengue virus develops severe dengue, a life-threatening medical emergency that requires immediate medical attention," according to YaleMedicine. Why is dengue called 'breakbone fever?' Dengue is sometimes called “breakbone fever” because, in addition to fever, "it can cause severe aches and pains, especially in the legs, joints, and back," according to YaleMedicine. Dengue does not actually cause bones to break, according to YaleMedicine. Is dengue contagious? Dengue is spread by mosquitoes. Mosquitoes become infected with dengue after biting a person with the virus. Those infected mosquitoes then spread the virus to other people through bites. The Centers for Disease Control said a woman infected with the dengue virus during pregnancy could pass the virus to her fetus, causing death to the fetus, low birth weight, or premature birth. Can dengue be treated? "While there is no cure for dengue and there are no specific medications for the condition, getting adequate hydration and plenty of rest, and taking acetaminophen for pain and fever can help alleviate symptoms while the body’s immune system fights the virus," according to YaleMedicine. "In most cases, people recover from dengue within a week. For those with severe dengue, treatment by health care professionals can significantly lower the risk of death." What are the best mosquito repellents to use? According to the Florida Department of Health, repellents with the following active ingredients provide the best protection: DEET Picaridin IR3535 Oil of lemon eucalyptus Para-menthane-diol 2-undecanone, a plant-derived ingredient 'Drain and cover' helps keep mosquitoes away The FDOH offered this advice to help keep mosquitoes away from your Florida home: Drain Drain water from garbage cans, old tires, pool covers, or any other containers where water collects. Empty and clean birdbaths and pet water bowls at least once or twice a week. Protect boats and vehicles from rain with tarps that don’t accumulate water. Maintain swimming pools in good condition and ensure they are appropriately chlorinated. Empty plastic swimming pools when not in use. Cover If you must be outside when mosquitoes are active, cover up. Apply mosquito repellent to exposed skin and clothing. Use mosquito netting to protect children younger than 2 months old. 佛罗里达州登革热 暴发 发生历史性转折,三个县宣布进入紧急状态 - https://latination.com/

Florida's dengue outbreak takes a historic turn as three counties declare emergencies - https://latination.com/
新闻简报佛罗里达州正面临自1930年代以来最严重的登革热暴发,三个县——希尔斯伯勒县、帕斯科县和皮尼拉斯县——已宣布进入紧急状态以应对这一危机。该暴发于2026年10月22日由疾控中心(CDC)确认,包括8个县的368例本地病例和288例旅行病例。坦帕湾都会区是主要的疫情中心,截至2026年10月30日,已报告456例本地确诊病例。公共卫生官员强调需要迅速采取登革热控制措施,包括加强杀虫剂喷洒和财产检查。建议采取预防措施,如清除积水和使用美国环保局(EPA)批准的驱蚊剂,以减少传播。此次暴发导致47%的本地患者需要住院治疗,并造成一例确诊病例死亡,促使卫生部门发布紧急健康警报和社区安全指南。紧急声明允许简化行政程序并获得紧急资金,目前仍在努力控制蚊虫种群并防止进一步的登革热传播。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发于2026年10月22日得到确认,紧急声明在2026年9月最后一周获得批准,并延长至2026年10月下旬。活动日期范围为2026年9月23日至10月29日,活动持续至2026年11月5日。
地点与对象
暴发 主要影响坦帕湾大都市区,特别是佛罗里达州的希尔斯伯勒县、帕斯科县和皮内拉斯县。受影响人口包括这些县的居民,重点是城市社区和私人业主。
事件
佛罗里达州经历了自 1930 年代以来最大规模的本地感染登革热 暴发,希尔斯伯勒县、帕斯科县和皮内拉斯县发布了紧急声明。CDC 在 2026 年 10 月 22 日的技术演示中确认了 暴发,其中包括 8 个县的 368 例本土病例和 288 例旅行病例。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 记录了 3 个县的 456 例本地确诊病例,其中希尔斯伯勒 385 例,皮内拉斯 67 例,帕斯科 4 例。全州 12 个县报告了 485 例本地病例。47%的当地感染患者需要立即住院治疗,并报告有1人确诊死亡。暴发 被描述为该州历史上最重要的本地登革热 传播 循环。
应对、状态与不确定性
紧急声明于 2026 年 9 月下旬获得批准,并延长至 2026 年 10 月下旬。加强了 媒介 控制行动,包括地面和空中杀幼虫剂 治疗。公共卫生官员发布了流行病学咨询和预防指南,敦促居民消除积水并采取防护措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Florida's dengue outbreak takes a historic turn as three counties declare emergencies - https://latination.com/

News Brief
Florida is facing its largest dengue outbreak since the 1930s, with three counties—Hillsborough, Pasco, and Pinellas—declaring emergencies to manage the crisis. The outbreak was confirmed by the CDC on October 22, 2026, and included 368 autochthonous cases across 8 counties and 288 travel cases. The Tampa Bay metropolitan area was the primary epicenter, with 456 confirmed local cases reported by October 30, 2026. Public health officials emphasized the need for rapid vector control operations, including intensified larvicide treatments and property inspections. Preventive measures such as eliminating standing water and using EPA-approved insect repellents were recommended to mitigate the spread. The outbreak has led to 47% of local patients requiring hospitalization and one confirmed death, prompting urgent health alerts and community safety guidelines. Emergency declarations allowed for streamlined administrative procedures and access to emergency funding, with ongoing efforts to control the mosquito population and prevent further transmission.

Local government agencies across the Tampa Bay area intensified ground and aerial larvicide treatments to suppress infected mosquito populations within residential zones. Public health specialists urge residents to recognize critical warning signs early while actively eliminating standing water sources around private properties. Unprecedented health alerts expanded rapidly across the state after official CDC reporting confirmed during a technical presentation on October 22, 2026, that Florida’s mosquito population triggered the largest dengue outbreak since the 1930s; furthermore, data compiled through October 15 detailed 368 autochthonous cases across 8 counties alongside 288 travel cases, while field inspectors identified 20 virus positive mosquito pools and registered that 47% of infected local patients required immediate hospitalization alongside 1 confirmed death: “This represents the most significant local dengue transmission cycle in state history.” Meanwhile, accelerating vector proliferation affected urban communities surrounding Tampa Bay, with Hillsborough County emerging as the primary epicenter; consequently, public health entomologists confirmed that Aedes aegypti and Aedes albopictus species continue breeding aggressively in artificial water containers, prompting regional environmental health departments to issue urgent epidemiological advisories across affected neighborhoods. Elimination of standing water: Homeowners must drain standing water receptacles weekly to prevent conditions requiring emergency declarations. Protective outdoor clothing: Individuals should wear loose long sleeved shirts and pants during day and night hours. Immediate medical evaluation: Severe abdominal pain or mucosal bleeding requires immediate emergency medical evaluation. Residential inspection clearance: Residents must grant full access to vector control teams evaluating local vector breeding sites. Clinical management and severe dengue symptom surveillance under emergency declarations Clinical manifestations of dengue fever typically present as high fever accompanied by headache, retro orbital pain, muscle aches, nausea, vomiting and rash lasting between 2 and 7 days; nevertheless, medical specialists warn that the critical phase of severe dengue often begins precisely when the acute fever starts to subside after approximately 1 week. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Florida Dengue Cases Reach 485 as Voters Weigh... - inkl

News Brief
Florida has confirmed 485 locally acquired dengue cases this year, marking the largest outbreak in the U.S. as of November 4, 2026. The state's latest arbovirus surveillance report, covering October 18 to 24, added 60 local cases, with 12 counties reporting local spread. This surpasses the previous high of 199 cases in 2023 and 62 cases in the prior year. The outbreak is under investigation, and the state is monitoring its progression. Meanwhile, voters are considering Amendment 3, a tax measure that could impact mosquito control programs, which requires 60% approval to pass. The article highlights the political context of the amendment, which aims to expand the homestead property tax exemption, potentially affecting government services and housing construction. The outbreak's scale and impact are significant, but no explicit risk level has been stated in the evidence provided.

登革热和手足口病在胡志明市正在复杂发展。- 越南.vn

Dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease are developing in a complicated manner in Ho Chi Minh City. - Vietnam.vn
新闻简报越南胡志明市正在经历 登革热 和手足口病复杂且令人担忧的上升趋势。截至最新报告,已记录超过 86,500 例病例,其中 16 人死亡,凸显了情况的严重性。仅登革热就已报告病例超过42,500例,其中死亡11例,而手足口病与去年同期相比增加了65%以上,病例超过44,000例。卫生部门针对“暴发”问题发起了一场高强度的运动,强调蚊虫控制、环境卫生和社区参与。手足口病病例中肠道病毒 71 型 (EV71) 的存在引起了人们的关注,因为它可能会导致儿童严重并发症。该市卫生部门正在敦促当地社区、学校和家庭采取积极措施,包括检查和消除蚊子滋生地、保持卫生、确保及时发现和及时发现病例。该运动还涉及与地方当局和公众的协调,以防止进一步传播并降低出现严重后果的风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
新闻报道发布于2026-11-02。事件日期没有明确说明,但描述的情况是在雨季期间并在托儿机构中发展起来的。
地点与对象
越南胡志明市。受影响人群包括学前班、幼儿园和日托中心的儿童以及广大社区。
事件
登革热和手足口病在胡志明市呈上升趋势,报告病例超过86,500例,死亡16人。据描述,情况复杂且令人担忧,有严重病例和死亡病例。
规模、影响与风险
胡志明市已报告登革热病例超过42,500例,其中死亡11例,手足口病病例超过44,000例,与去年同期相比增加了65%以上。传播风险仍然存在,特别是在幼儿高度集中的地区,而肠道病毒 71 型 (EV71) 的存在因其潜在的严重并发症而加剧了人们的担忧。
应对、状态与不确定性
胡志明市卫生部门正在开展高强度的暴发防控活动,重点是环境卫生、蚊虫控制和社区参与。该活动包括喷洒灭蚊剂、家庭指导以及学校和日托中心的监测。应对工作持续进行,落实责任,加大检查督导力度,确保落实有效。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease are developing in a complicated manner in Ho Chi Minh City. - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
Ho Chi Minh City, Vietnam, is experiencing a complex and concerning rise in dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease. As of the latest report, over 86,500 cases have been recorded, with 16 deaths, highlighting the severity of the situation. Dengue fever alone has seen over 42,500 cases, including 11 deaths, while hand, foot, and mouth disease has increased by over 65% compared to the same period last year, with more than 44,000 cases. The health sector has launched a high-intensity campaign to address the outbreaks, emphasizing mosquito control, environmental sanitation, and community engagement. The presence of Enterovirus 71 (EV71) in hand, foot, and mouth disease cases raises concerns due to its potential to cause serious complications in children. The city's health authorities are urging local communities, schools, and households to take proactive measures, including inspecting and eliminating mosquito breeding grounds, maintaining hygiene, and ensuring timely detection and treatment of cases. The campaign also involves coordination with local authorities and the public to prevent further spread and reduce the risk of severe outcomes.

Notably, the number of cases is trending upwards again, as the rainy season weather continues to create favorable conditions for disease-transmitting mosquitoes to breed and develop. Both dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease are on the rise in Ho Chi Minh City. At the same time, hand, foot, and mouth disease also recorded a complex situation with over 44,000 cases, an increase of over 65% compared to the same period last year. The city recorded 5 deaths and more than 1,600 outbreaks, indicating that the risk of spread remains, especially in areas with a high concentration of young children. One of the factors causing concern in the healthcare sector is the circulation of Enterovirus 71 (EV71), a strain of virus capable of causing serious neurological, cardiovascular, and respiratory complications in children with hand, foot, and mouth disease. Meanwhile, the number of cases appearing in preschools and kindergartens is on the rise, increasing the risk of transmission in group settings. Khám phá thêm Tìm hiểu lịch sử Đọc Mẹo Du Lịch Khám phá ĐNA Given these developments, the Ho Chi Minh City health sector has determined that controlling both diseases simultaneously is an urgent task, requiring the participation of local authorities, healthcare facilities, schools, and individual households. The leaders of the Ho Chi Minh City Department of Health stated that the figures on cases and deaths serve as a warning regarding epidemic prevention and control efforts. Local authorities and the public need to increase vigilance, avoiding complacency or delays in detecting and addressing the risk of infection. In addition, untreated mosquito breeding grounds, unsanitary living environments, or cases of disease that go undetected can all increase the risk of disease outbreaks spreading in the community. For dengue fever, prevention efforts should focus on controlling disease vectors, eliminating mosquito breeding grounds, and early detection of infected individuals. For hand, foot, and mouth disease, close monitoring in schools, preschools, and daycare centers is crucial to limit the risk of outbreaks. You may also like Faced with the simultaneous increase in two diseases, the Ho Chi Minh City Department of Health, in coordination with the People's Committee of Di An Ward, launched a high-intensity campaign to prevent and control dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease, focusing on mobilizing the participation of the political system and the people in controlling risk factors. During the campaign, local authorities were instructed to strengthen guidance and organize comprehensive environmental sanitation, and to review and address areas at risk of disease outbreaks. Specific responsibilities were assigned, coupled with increased inspection and supervision to ensure that disease prevention measures were implemented regularly and effectively. The health sector continues to strengthen epidemiological surveillance, early detection of cases, timely handling of outbreaks, and guidance for the public on recognizing warning signs of severe illness. Communication efforts are also emphasized to raise awareness of disease prevention, reduce self-treatment, and minimize delays in bringing patients to medical facilities. Specifically, the Ho Chi Minh City Department of Health requires that mosquito control spraying must be carried out according to professional indications, targeting the correct insects and following proper technical procedures. Local authorities are prohibited from spraying chemicals indiscriminately or considering this measure as a substitute for eliminating mosquito larvae and removing water containers that pose a breeding ground for mosquitoes. For hand, foot, and mouth disease, preschools and daycare centers need to strengthen daily health monitoring of children, strictly implement hygiene measures for classrooms, toys, equipment, and frequently touched surfaces. Coordination between schools and parents must be maintained to promptly detect children showing suspected symptoms and minimize the risk of cross-infection. In parallel with the activities of the health sector, a network of community health collaborators is mobilized to go directly to each household, lodging area, and construction site to disseminate information, provide guidance on environmental hygiene, address stagnant water sources, and implement disease prevention measures. The Department of Health leaders urged each family to take time at least once a week to inspect, rinse, and cover water containers, remove discarded items that could collect water, and proactively eliminate mosquito larvae. In addition, people should maintain the habit of washing hands with soap, cleaning children's toys and household items, and limiting contact with sources of infection. Regarding proactive disease prevention measures, Dr. Le Thi Giao Thi from Long Chau Pharmacy and Vaccination System stated that, in addition to environmental hygiene, controlling sources of infection, and early symptom detection, vaccination is a crucial solution to reduce the risk of disease and dangerous complications. Especially in the context of the simultaneous increase in dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease, proactively immunizing appropriate target groups is even more significant in protecting public health. For dengue fever, Vietnam has now implemented the Qdenga vaccine, which is indicated for people aged 4 years and older, with a two-dose regimen administered 3 months apart. You may also like The vaccine was developed to prevent disease caused by all four types of Dengue virus, contributing to a reduction in the risk of illness and hospitalization. It is important to note that people who have had dengue fever are still at risk of reinfection by other types of the virus; therefore, appropriate vaccination advice is necessary, rather than assuming that having had the disease provides long-lasting immunity. With regard to hand, foot, and mouth disease, Vietnam's approval of the Envacgen vaccine against the EV71 virus in 2026 has opened up another preventive option for young children. This virus strain can cause serious complications such as encephalitis, myocarditis, and acute pulmonary edema. The vaccine is indicated for children from 2 months to under 6 years old, helping to prevent disease caused by EV71. However, because hand, foot, and mouth disease is associated with many other virus strains, vaccination cannot replace personal hygiene measures, disinfecting toys and classrooms, and monitoring children's health daily. Experts advise parents to proactively seek information, take their children for screening, and ensure they receive vaccinations at the appropriate age and with the correct dosage as recommended by medical professionals. The combination of vaccination, source control, and early detection of severe symptoms will help reduce the risk of hospitalization, complications, and death from these two increasingly prevalent infectious diseases. 登革热 暴发袭击中国海南建筑工地,数千名工人被隔离

Dengue Fever Outbreak Hits Construction Sites in China’s Hainan, Thousands of Workers Quarantined
新闻简报据报道,中国海南省三亚市的多个建筑工地出现了 暴发 病毒,影响了大约 4,000 至 5,000 名工人。据当地工人介绍,济阳区“三亚保利天骏”住宅项目发生暴发,导致工人被转移到酒店和住宅小区进行隔离。中国市政府接管了这一情况,实施封锁并在现场实施 24 小时警察驻守。虽然济阳区卫生健康委员会表示,所有工人的检测结果均为阴性,但具体感染病例数仍不清楚。暴发 引起了居民和工人的担忧,一些人由于过去的漏报情况而对官方数据表示不信任。环境消毒、灭蚊等措施已经启动,但暴发的有效性和全面性仍在调查中。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发 暴发报告时间为2026年10月下旬,情况持续至2026年11月。发表日期为2026-11-03,事件日期未明确说明,但推测为2026年10月31日左右。
地点与对象
暴发 事件发生在中国海南省三亚市,特别是济阳区的建筑工地。大约 4,000 至 5,000 名工人受到影响。
事件
中国海南省三亚市的多个建筑工地报告了 暴发 病毒,导致数千名工人被隔离。该事件得到了中国媒体和当地工作人员的证实。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 影响了数千名工人,其中一些人被转移到酒店和住宅区进行隔离。该风险与 重症登革热 的可能性相关,包括登革出血热或登革休克综合征,但尚未报告 重症登革热 确诊病例。感染病例的确切数量仍不清楚。
应对、状态与不确定性
当局已实施检疫措施、接触者追踪和环境消毒。但由于缺乏官方数据,具体病例数和措施的有效性尚不确定。应对措施包括封锁、24 小时警察驻守以及对工人进行重复验血。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dengue Fever Outbreak Hits Construction Sites in China’s Hainan, Thousands of Workers Quarantined

News Brief
An outbreak of dengue fever has been reported at several construction sites in Sanya, Hainan Province, China, affecting approximately 4,000 to 5,000 workers. According to local workers, the outbreak occurred at the 'Sanya Poly Tianjun' residential project in the Jiyang District, leading to the relocation of workers to hotels and residential complexes for quarantine. The Chinese municipal government took over the situation, implementing lockdowns and 24-hour police presence at the sites. While the Health Commission of Jiyang District stated that all test results for the workers were negative, the exact number of infected cases remains unknown. The outbreak has raised concerns among residents and workers, with some expressing distrust in official data due to past instances of underreporting. Measures such as environmental disinfection and mosquito control have been initiated, but the effectiveness and full extent of the outbreak are still under investigation.
Original source text (English)
This copy is for your personal, non-commercial use only. Distribution and use of this material are governed by our Subscriber Agreement and by copyright law. For non-personal use or to order multiple copies, please contact The Epoch Times Reprints. Open sidebar AD Social Issues Dengue Fever Outbreak Hits Construction Sites in China’s Hainan, Thousands of Workers Quarantined Workers said that dozens have been infected, thousands have been relocated for quarantine, and several construction sites are locked down. Save Mark Us Preferred on Google Print Beaches are empty as the area is closed off and restricted due to an outbreak of COVID-19 in Sanya in southern China's Hainan Province on Aug. 6, 2022. CNS/AFP via Getty Images Alex Wu 11/3/2026|Updated: 11/4/2026 An outbreak of dengue fever at several high-end residential construction sites in Sanya city in Hainan, China’s southernmost island province, has resulted in thousands being quarantined, according to Chinese media and local workers who spoke with The Epoch Times. State-controlled Chinese media outlet The Beijing News reported on Oct. 31 that a dengue fever outbreak recently hit construction sites in Sanya, the famous tourist site located on the southern tip of Hainan island. According to the report, some of the workers have been relocated to local hotels or residential complexes for quarantine and testing—a move that has sparked discontent among residents who were not informed beforehand. Local construction workers told The Epoch Times the outbreak occurred at “Sanya Poly Tianjun,” a high-end residential project in the Jiyang District of Sanya city. Multiple construction sites within the project were affected, impacting thousands of workers, they said, and construction at several sites has been suspended, with both living quarters and construction areas placed under lockdown. The CDC defines dengue fever as an acute infectious disease caused by the dengue virus and transmitted through the bites of infected “Aedes” mosquitoes. It is not transmitted directly from person to person. Following infection, the incubation period typically ranges from 1 to 14 days. Symptoms include sudden onset of fever, headache, pain behind the eyes, and muscle and joint pain. In severe cases, it may progress to dengue hemorrhagic fever or dengue shock syndrome, both of which can be life-threatening. Sun Zhiyong, a worker still at the construction site who uses a pseudonym for fear of reprisal from the Chinese regime, told The Epoch Times the site had previously been placed under lockdown for about a week due to an outbreak of dengue fever, affecting approximately 1,000 people. “Some of us are under quarantine within the living quarters at the construction site, while all personnel who were living off-site have been moved to the Beauty Crown Hotel,” a landmark resort in Sanya. Related Stories Villagers and Police Clash After Launch of Hainan Free Trade Port, Residents Say One Month After Launch, Hainan Free Trade Port Remains CCP’s Internal Experiment: Analysts Sun said nearly 20 busloads of workers have been sent to the hotel, with each construction site relocating about 800 to 1,000 people. He added that the situation is not limited to a single site, as several construction projects are clustered in the same area, though they are managed by different contractors. “When an outbreak occurs at one site, operations at all the surrounding sites are halted. In total, there are about 4,000 to 5,000 workers affected,” Sun said, adding that following the outbreak, the workers were instructed to remain in their living quarters for quarantine. “There must be dozens of infected cases at my construction site; otherwise, the measures wouldn’t be this strict,” he said. Sun also said that some infected workers were sent directly to the hospital, though the exact number remains unknown to outsiders. “The authorities haven’t released any figures,” he said. He said his construction site is not the only place where an outbreak has occurred, and other areas in the city have also implemented epidemic prevention and control measures. In the early stages of the outbreak, the project developers did not attach sufficient importance to the situation, according to Sun. “After the Sanya municipal government took over, the developers began to take it seriously. Police were stationed at the entrance 24 hours a day, preventing workers from entering or leaving. The mayor of Sanya even visited the site in person to give instructions.” Meanwhile, various construction sites have initiated environmental disinfection and mosquito and pest control measures, according to Sun. Zhou Dafu, another worker from the Hainan branch of China Construction Second Engineering Bureau Co., Ltd., who uses a pseudonym for fear of reprisal, told The Epoch Times that the site where he was working had been placed under lockdown for two days because of the dengue fever outbreak. “We were all quarantined within the living quarters,” he said. In addition to spraying disinfectant on the ground, drones flying below rooftop level were deployed to spray chemicals at the site, Zhou said. Sports cars are parked in front of luxury apartment blocks on Jan. 19, 2013, at the seaside city of Sanya, in China's southern Hainan province. Wang Zhao/AFP/Getty Images Repeated Blood Tests During the quarantine, Sun said he underwent three blood tests, while Zhou said he was tested five times. Zhao Jianguo, another worker who has since left the construction site and uses a pseudonym out of fear of reprisal, told The Epoch Times that hundreds of workers at his site were required to line up for blood tests following the outbreak. Then, due to mandatory repeated testing, excessive wait times, and the fact that they received no wages while work was suspended, he ultimately decided to leave, Zhao said. “I’ve already left. I didn’t dare to stay or go to work there anymore,” he said. Workers are worried about getting infected and are unwilling to continue taking the risk of showing up for work, Zhao said. “With the outbreak this severe, who would still dare to work there?” Relocated to Residential Complex for Quarantine Hundreds of workers from a nearby construction site have been relocated to the “Sanya Evergrande Beauty Crown” residential complex in the Jiyang District of Sanya because of the dengue outbreak, according to The Beijing News’ report. A homeowner surnamed Fan told The Beijing News that neither the property management nor the relevant authorities provided advance notice—nor did they seek input from residents—regarding the arrival and stay of these workers. Concerned that her family might become infected—especially given that she has an infant at home—she has not dared to open her doors or windows for several days, not even those equipped with screens, according to the report. Residents and tourists line up for COVID-19 PCR tests in Sanya in southern China's Hainan Province on Aug. 8, 2022. STR/AFP via Getty Images A staff member at the Sanya Center for Disease Control and Prevention told The Beijing News that all workers have been moved out of the residential complex. The Health Commission of Jiyang District also said that all test results for the workers during their period of isolation were negative. However, because of the Chinese communist regime’s record of publishing unreliable data and downplaying situations it deemed unfavorable to its rule, such as the COVID-19 infections and related deaths since early 2020, the Chinese public lacks trust in the official statements. Social media posts about the dengue fever outbreak in Sanya have continued to circulate, with some of them saying that centralized quarantine measures were being implemented in Sanya’s Jiyang District, and workers who had already left have been called back for quarantine. Li Jing and Gu Xiaohua contributed to this report. We had a problem loading this article. Please enable javascript or use a different browser. If the issue persists, please visit our help center. 随着病例上升,阿富汗东部两名年轻人死于 登革热 - KabulNow

Two Young Men Die of Dengue Fever in Eastern Afghanistan as Cases Rise - KabulNow

Two Young Men Die of Dengue Fever in Eastern Afghanistan as Cases Rise - KabulNow

News Brief
Two young men have died of dengue fever in Eastern Afghanistan, specifically in Jalalabad and Momand Dara district, over the past two days, according to local sources reported by KabulNow. The deaths occurred on October 31 and November 1, 2026, as dengue cases rise in the region. Residents in Nangarhar province have expressed concern over the increasing number of cases and the lack of sufficient containment measures or adequate treatment. They warn that the situation could worsen without urgent public health interventions. In response, the Taliban-appointed director of public health in Nangarhar, Mawlawi Aminullah Sharif, mentioned that mosquito nets are being distributed and mobile health teams have been sent to several districts to provide treatment. However, the ongoing challenges in the healthcare system, including funding shortages and restrictions on women’s participation in healthcare, have contributed to the difficulty in managing the outbreak effectively.

Podcast Submission About us Provided Besmellah Zahidi·November 2, 2026 Two Young Men Die of Dengue Fever in Eastern Afghanistan as Cases Rise KABUL, AFGHANISTAN – Two young men have died of dengue fever in Afghanistan’s eastern Nangarhar province over the past two days, local sources told KabulNow, as cases rise amid limited public health efforts to contain the disease. The two men died in Jalalabad, the provincial capital, and Momand Dara district, the sources said. Both had been receiving treatment in the infectious diseases department of a local hospital for several weeks, but their conditions did not improve. One died on Sunday, November 1, and the other on Saturday, October 31, according to the sources. The deaths have raised concern among residents in Nangarhar, who say the number of dengue cases is increasing and that sufficient steps have not yet been taken to contain the outbreak or ensure adequate treatment. Residents have warned that the situation could worsen unless urgent public health measures are introduced to contain the outbreak and improve access to medical care. Mawlawi Aminullah Sharif, the Taliban-appointed director of public health in Nangarhar, said mosquito nets were being distributed in Jalalabad and the province’s districts as part of efforts to prevent the disease. He added that mobile health teams and medical equipment had been dispatched to several districts to provide treatment. More on this story Dengue fever is a viral disease transmitted to humans through infected Aedes mosquitoes. According to the World Health Organization (WHO), symptoms include high fever, headache, and body aches, while severe cases can be life-threatening. Nangarhar province records a significant number of dengue-related deaths each year. Local residents and sources attribute the ongoing toll in part to shortages of medical facilities and challenges in the response. Afghanistan’s healthcare system has faced mounting challenges since the Taliban returned to power in August 2021, with reduced international assistance, economic difficulties, and the return of millions of Afghans from neighboring countries adding to pressure on medical services. Hundreds of health facilities have closed or reduced their services in recent years, largely because of funding shortages. Restrictions imposed by the Taliban on women’s participation in healthcare and humanitarian work have also affected the delivery of essential services. The WHO and other humanitarian organizations have repeatedly called for increased international support to sustain basic healthcare services, strengthen disease surveillance, and improve Afghanistan’s capacity to prevent and respond to outbreaks. Residents have warned that the situation could worsen unless urgent public health measures are introduced to contain the outbreak and improve access to medical care.
Response, status and uncertainty
The Taliban-appointed director of public health in Nangarhar, Mawlawi Aminullah Sharif, stated that mosquito nets were being distributed in Jalalabad and the province’s districts as part of efforts to prevent the disease. Mobile health teams and medical equipment had been dispatched to several districts to provide treatment.

佛罗里达州 登革热 病例激增 - NPR

Florida sees spike in dengue fever cases - NPR
新闻简报佛罗里达州的 登革热 病例大幅增加,今年报告了 400 多例本地感染病例,主要发生在该州。暴发被描述为史无前例,与一名老年妇女的死亡有关,并由于气候变化和埃及伊蚊的存在等因素而蔓延。这些蚊子具有抗旱能力,在经历了长期干旱后,于六月孵化,导致其数量在炎热潮湿的夏季激增。这种传播在坦帕等地区更为明显,与佛罗里达州南部各县相比,这些地区对热带疾病的准备程度较低。虽然随着凉爽、干燥天气的到来,每周病例数开始下降,但情况仍在调查中,研究人员正在研究气候变化在美国登革热 传播 中的作用。暴发 强调了气候变暖地区热带疾病的威胁日益严重。

地点与对象
美国佛罗里达州,病例主要在该州报告,尤其是坦帕。
事件
佛罗里达州今年的 登革热 病例数量创历史新高,迄今为止已报告 400 多例本地病例,几乎全部发生在佛罗里达州。暴发 与一名老年妇女的死亡有关,并且由于气候变化和埃及伊蚊的存在等因素而正在传播。
规模、影响与风险
美国已报告 400 多例本地病例,几乎所有病例都发生在佛罗里达州。暴发 被描述为前所未有的事件,并且与气候变化有关,气候变化增加了登革热 传播 的风险。这种传播归因于埃及伊蚊及其在六月孵化的抗旱卵的存在,导致炎热潮湿的夏季蚊子数量增加。
应对、状态与不确定性
随着佛罗里达州空气凉爽、干燥,每周病例数正在放缓。该州尚未做好充分准备来应对登革热的传播,特别是在坦帕等地区。研究正在进行中,正在研究气候变化对美国登革热 传播 的影响。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Florida sees spike in dengue fever cases - NPR

News Brief
Florida is witnessing a significant increase in dengue fever cases, with over 400 local infections reported this year, primarily in the state. The outbreak, described as unprecedented, is linked to the death of an elderly woman and is spreading due to factors such as climate change and the presence of Aedes aegypti mosquitoes. These mosquitoes, which are drought-resistant, hatched in June after a long dry period, leading to a surge in their population during the hot, humid summer. The spread is more pronounced in areas like Tampa, where preparedness for tropical diseases is lower compared to southern Florida counties. While weekly cases are beginning to decline with the arrival of cooler, drier weather, the situation remains under investigation, and researchers are studying the role of climate change in the transmission of dengue in the U.S. The outbreak highlights the growing threat of tropical diseases in regions experiencing warming climates.
Original source text (English)

Health Florida sees spike in dengue fever cases October 30, 20264:47 AM ET Heard on Morning Edition From Jessica Meszaros Florida sees spike in dengue fever cases Listen· 2:462-Minute ListenPlaylist Transcript Toggle more options Download Embed Embed <iframe src="https://www.npr.org/player/embed/nx-s1-6020514/nx-s1-9984834" width="100%" height="290" frameborder="0" scrolling="no" title="NPR embedded audio player"> Transcript Florida is seeing a record number of dengue fever cases this year as the tropical disease thrives in warming climates. Transcript STEVE INSKEEP, HOST: Some other news now. Florida has the largest outbreak of locally acquired dengue in the continental U.S. in nearly a century. It's linked to the death of an elderly woman, and it is spreading. Here's Jessica Meszaros with our member station WUSF. JESSICA MESZAROS, BYLINE: Infected mosquitoes spread dengue to people. Symptoms include high fever, headache and body pain, which is how it got the nickname break-bone fever. People typically contract it while traveling through a continent where the virus is always present, like South America, Asia or Africa. But this year, folks who got infected during their worldly adventures brought it back to the U.S., passing it on to the mosquitoes here. Now over 400 local cases have been reported in the U.S. so far, almost entirely in Florida. EVA BUCKNER: I have not seen anything like this. It is very much unprecedented. MESZAROS: Eva Buckner is an insect scientist at the University of Florida. She says one factor in this spread is dengue has been recently really active globally. BUCKNER: Which allows multiple travel cases to occur in Florida. MESZAROS: Plus, the state already has the type of mosquito that can carry dengue - Aedes aegypti. And then there was a long drought this year. BUCKNER: Aedes aegypti has drought-resistant eggs, and so it allowed some of the eggs to build up in the environment. MESZAROS: So when it finally rained in June, all those eggs hatched simultaneously - just in time for a hot, humid summer, which these mosquitoes thrive in. In Tampa, cases spiked because the area wasn't prepared to fight this tropical disease like they already do in more southern Florida counties. Erin Mordecai is an infectious disease researcher at Stanford University. She co-authored a study published last year that found a warming climate can increase the number of dengue transmissions. ERIN MORDECAI: We estimate that about 18% of the global dengue burden is attributable to climate change that had already happened by 1995 to 2014, so not even including the last decade of warming. MESZAROS: Climate change increases the chance of temperatures reaching 84 degrees in more places, which is optimal for dengue transmissions. This analysis was done on 21 countries in Latin America and Asia. But Mordecai says they're currently studying the U.S. For now, the good news is weekly cases of dengue are slowing down a bit in Florida as the state welcomes cooler, drier air. For NPR News, I'm Jessica Meszaros in Tampa, Florida. (SOUNDBITE OF BRANT BJORK'S "WAITING FOR A COCONUT TO DROP") Copyright © 2026 NPR. All rights reserved. Event
Florida is experiencing a record number of dengue fever cases this year, with over 400 local cases reported so far, almost entirely in Florida. The outbreak is linked to the death of an elderly woman and is spreading due to factors like climate change and the presence of Aedes aegypti mosquitoes.

新闻简报The news report highlights several studies conducted on November 4, 2026, focusing on different health-related topics. A Catalan registry study found that anti-CGRP antibody treatment reduced monthly migraine days by about 45 percent among 5438 patients. In Eastern Uganda, a survey of 92 nurses at Mbale Regional Referral Hospital revealed that 61.96 percent adhered fully to the Five Rights of antimicrobial administration. An 81-sample groundwater survey in Gujarat's Porbandar district showed varying water quality, with the poorest along the Arabian Sea coast. These findings are based on reported data and require further verification for their implications.

The longitudinal study in China found a 56 percent link between higher cognitive reserve and slower language decline.

孟加拉国登革热 暴发 2026:10 月死亡人数上升,预计 12 月将出现缓解 - https://www.businesstimes-bd.com/

Bangladesh Dengue Outbreak 2026: October Deaths Rise, Relief Expected in December - https://www.businesstimes-bd.com/
新闻简报In Bangladesh, a dengue outbreak has resulted in six additional deaths and the hospitalization of 1,866 new patients within the 24 hours ending at 8:00 on 05 November 2026. The report, published on 31 October 2026 by the Business Times-BD, highlights the severity of the outbreak, with a significant number of cases reported across multiple regions including Dhaka, Chittagong, Rajshahi, Khulna, Barisal, Sylhet, Rangpur, and Mymensingh. The outbreak has caused a substantial public health impact, with the high number of hospitalizations indicating a critical situation. The government is expected to provide relief in December, but no specific actions or responses are detailed in the report. The situation remains under investigation, and the evidence is based on reported hospitalizations and deaths. The article does not explicitly state the risk level, but the scale of the outbreak suggests a serious threat to the population.
查看新闻五要素
时间
The outbreak was reported in the 24 hours ending at 8:00 on 05 November 2026. The article was published on 31 October 2026.
地点与对象
暴发 发生在孟加拉国,特别是在达卡、吉大港、拉杰沙希、库尔纳、巴里萨尔、锡尔赫特、朗布尔和迈门辛。受影响的人群包括因登革热住院的人。
事件
登革病毒 在孟加拉国造成另外 6 人死亡,并导致 1,866 名新患者住院。孟加拉国媒体“Business Times-BD”对此进行了报道。
规模、影响与风险
新增 6 例死亡病例,1,866 名新患者入院。文章没有明确说明风险级别,但住院人数众多表明对公共健康产生了重大影响。
应对、状态与不确定性
文章提到政府预计将在12月提供救助,但没有详细说明具体行动或应对措施。目前情况正在调查中,证据基于报告的住院和死亡情况。

Time
The outbreak was reported in the 24 hours ending at 8:00 on 05 November 2026. The article was published on 31 October 2026.
Location and population
The outbreak occurred in Bangladesh, specifically in Dhaka, Chittagong, Rajshahi, Khulna, Barisal, Sylhet, Rangpur, and Mymensingh. The affected population includes individuals hospitalized due to dengue.
Event
Dengue virus caused six additional deaths and hospitalized 1,866 new patients in Bangladesh. This was reported by the Bangladesh media outlet 'Business Times-BD'.
Scale, impact and risk
Six more deaths were recorded, and 1,866 new patients were admitted to hospitals. The article does not explicitly state the risk level, but the high number of hospitalizations indicates a significant public health impact.
Response, status and uncertainty
The article mentions that the government is expected to provide relief in December, but no specific actions or responses are detailed. The situation is under investigation, and the evidence is based on reported hospitalizations and deaths.

孟加拉国登革热月死亡人数达 418 人沙巴日报 - 沙巴日报

Bangladesh records deadliest dengue month as deaths reach 418 | Daily Sabah - Daily Sabah
新闻简报2026 年 10 月,孟加拉国经历了登革热死亡人数最多的一个月,卫生部报告称,有 418 人死亡,超过 133,000 人入院治疗。病例激增给本已紧张的医疗保健系统带来了巨大压力,因为许多患者只是在出现严重并发症后才到达医院。卫生专家将死亡人数的上升归因于所有四种 登革病毒 血清型的循环,这可能导致更严重的感染,特别是对于重复感染的个体。预计这种情况将持续到 11 月,预计 12 月之前只会略有缓解。当局正在考虑引入登革热 疫苗 作为长期解决方案。暴发 还与气候变化有关,气候变化延长了蚊子的繁殖季节,并使伊蚊的活动时间更长。尽管面临挑战,我们仍在努力通过改进 诊断、治疗 和公众意识活动来管理危机。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 10 月,暴发 可能会持续到 11 月,并且可能要到 12 月才会显着缓解(N13、N14、N34、N35、N38)。
地点与对象
孟加拉国,特别是达卡,有 279 名登革热患者在 Shaheed Suhrawardy 医学院和医院(N13、N14、N19)接受 治疗 治疗。
事件
孟加拉国卫生部报告称,2026 年 10 月是孟加拉国登革热死亡人数最多的一个月,共有 418 人死亡,超过 133,000 人入院治疗(N13、N14、N16、N17、N18)。
规模、影响与风险
2026 年 10 月,报告了 418 例登革热死亡和超过 133,000 例住院患者。健康专家警告说,所有四种 登革病毒 血清型的传播都会导致更严重的感染,重复感染会增加严重并发症的风险(N13、N14、N16、N29、N30、N38)。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生专家正在监测局势,并警告称,每月死亡人数可能不会继续以同样的速度上升,但 传播 可能会在 11 月保持较高水平。当局正在考虑引入登革热 疫苗 作为一项长期措施(N13、N14、N22、N32、N33、N38)。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bangladesh records deadliest dengue month as deaths reach 418 | Daily Sabah - Daily Sabah

News Brief
In October 2026, Bangladesh experienced its deadliest month for dengue, with 418 deaths and over 133,000 hospital admissions reported by the Health Ministry. This surge in cases has placed significant pressure on the already strained healthcare system, as many patients arrive at hospitals only after developing severe complications. Health experts attribute the rise in deaths to the circulation of all four dengue virus serotypes, which can lead to more severe infections, especially in individuals with repeat infections. The situation is expected to persist through November, with only modest relief anticipated before December. Authorities are considering introducing a dengue vaccine as a long-term solution. The outbreak has also been linked to climate change, which is extending mosquito breeding seasons and keeping Aedes mosquitoes active for longer periods. Despite the challenges, efforts are ongoing to manage the crisis through improved diagnosis, treatment, and public awareness campaigns.

Bangladesh recorded its deadliest month of the year for dengue in October, with 173 deaths as health experts warned that the outbreak is likely to persist through November and may not ease significantly until December. The country reported 418 dengue deaths and more than 133,000 hospital admissions between Jan. 1 and Oct. 30, according to Health Ministry data. October's toll surpassed September's 148 deaths, making it the deadliest month of 2026 so far. The surge comes as Bangladesh is also struggling with the world's worst measles outbreak, which has killed more than 1,200 people, placing additional pressure on an already overstretched health care system. Doctors said many dengue patients were arriving at hospitals only after developing serious complications, making treatment more difficult. At the state-run Shaheed Suhrawardy Medical College and Hospital in Dhaka, 279 dengue patients were receiving treatment after 67 new admissions in a single day, hospital director Mirza Golam Sarwar said. "We are getting many patients in shock. Their blood pressure sometimes is too low to measure, and their oxygen levels are dropping," he said. Medicine specialist ABM Abdullah said repeat infections, coupled with delays in diagnosis and treatment, were contributing to the rise in severe cases and deaths. People who have previously contracted dengue face a greater risk of serious illness if infected again, he said, urging residents to eliminate mosquito breeding sites and seek medical attention as soon as symptoms appear. The danger of repeated infections is a growing concern for residents such as Dhaka's Faisal Ferdous, who has contracted dengue three times, in 2017, 2024 and again earlier this month. "This time, both my wife and I needed hospital treatment," said Ferdous, who is now recovering at home. He said the couple fell ill shortly after spending several days at a hospital where their 5-year-old son was receiving treatment for an unrelated illness. "Every bout has been difficult. After getting it repeatedly, you become increasingly concerned about the risk of severe illness," he said. Health experts have also warned that the circulation of all four dengue virus serotypes is contributing to more severe infections. People previously infected with one serotype can develop more serious complications when subsequently infected with another. Bangladesh has yet to introduce a dengue vaccine, although authorities are considering vaccination as a longer-term measure to tackle the disease. Despite the October surge, entomologist Kabirul Bashar of Jahangirnagar University said the monthly death toll was unlikely to continue rising at the same pace. However, he warned that dengue transmission would probably remain high through November. "We may see some relief, but dengue is unlikely to disappear in November. Any decline is likely to be modest, with significant improvement unlikely before December," he said. Bashar said mosquitoes would continue emerging from breeding sites created by earlier rainfall, allowing transmission to persist even as weather conditions become drier. He also pointed to changing rainfall and humidity patterns, partly linked to climate change, which are extending mosquito breeding seasons and allowing Aedes mosquitoes, the primary carriers of dengue, to remain active for longer. The prolonged transmission season, combined with repeat infections and mounting pressure on hospitals, means Bangladesh is unlikely to see a substantial improvement in its dengue situation before the end of the year. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

高强度开展“中小病”和手足口病防治行动。- 越南.vn

Launching a high-intensity campaign to prevent and control dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease. - Vietnam.vn
新闻简报2026年10月31日,胡志明市卫生厅针对登革热和手足口病病例激增和暴发风险开展高强度防控活动。截至10月30日的监测数据显示,登革热病例超过42,500例,其中死亡11例,手足口病病例超过44,000例,比2025年同期增加65%,死亡5例,1,600多例暴发。EV71 病毒株可引起严重并发症,是学前班特别关注的问题。该活动的重点是消除蚊子滋生地、改善学校卫生以及对公众进行预防教育。地方当局正在进行现场调查和重新检查,以解决风险区域,卫生部门正在与教育和家长协调,以确保安全的环境。登革热和手足口病仍然没有具体的治疗,强调需要早期发现和及时干预。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该活动于2026年10月31日启动,监测数据截至2026年10月30日,表明病例激增。预计雨季和年底有进一步增加的风险。
地点与对象
越南胡志明市,重点关注蚊子滋生地和疾病 传播 风险普遍存在的学前班、学校、日托中心和住宅区。
事件
胡志明市卫生部以病例激增和暴发风险为由,开展了高强度的行动来预防和控制登革热和手足口病。
规模、影响与风险
截至2026年10月30日,已记录超过42,500例登革热病例,包括11例死亡。手足口病病例超过44,000例,与去年同期相比增加了65%,有5例死亡和超过1,600例暴发被检测到。EV71病毒株是关注学前儿童严重并发症的病毒。
应对、状态与不确定性
该活动包括环境卫生工作、消灭蚊子幼虫、学校健康监测以及预防措施的公众教育。当地有关部门正在开展现场调查和重新检查,以解决风险区域。卫生部门正在与教育和家长协调,以确保安全的学习环境。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Launching a high-intensity campaign to prevent and control dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease. - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
On October 31, 2026, the Ho Chi Minh City Department of Health launched a high-intensity campaign to prevent and control dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease, responding to a surge in cases and the risk of outbreaks. Monitoring data up to October 30 shows over 42,500 dengue fever cases, including 11 deaths, and over 44,000 hand, foot, and mouth disease cases, a 65% increase from the same period in 2025, with 5 deaths and over 1,600 outbreaks. The EV71 virus strain, which can cause severe complications, is a particular concern in preschools. The campaign focuses on eliminating mosquito breeding grounds, improving hygiene in schools, and educating the public on prevention. Local authorities are conducting on-site surveys and re-inspections to address risk areas, and the health sector is coordinating with education and parents to ensure a safe environment. Dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease remain without specific treatments, emphasizing the need for early detection and timely intervention.
Original source text (English)
Launching a high-intensity campaign to prevent and control dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease. In the context of a surge in dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease cases in recent months, and forecasts of continued increases in the coming months of the year, on October 31st, the Ho Chi Minh City Department of Health organized the launching ceremony for the "High-Intensity Campaign for the Prevention and Control of Dengue Fever and Hand, Foot, and Mouth Disease in 2026". Báo Tin Tức • 31/10/2026 Children with hand, foot, and mouth disease are being treated at Children's Hospital 2 in Ho Chi Minh City. Photo: Dan Phuong/News and Ethnic Groups Newspaper The campaign was launched urgently to proactively control the disease situation and prevent the risk of outbreaks and spread in the city during the rainy season and the end of the year. Khám phá thêm Tìm hiểu lịch sử Đọc Mẹo Du Lịch Khám phá ĐNA According to Dr. Huynh Minh Chin, Deputy Director of the Ho Chi Minh City Department of Health , dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease are two infectious diseases that frequently circulate in Ho Chi Minh City, with a high risk of rapid increase during periods favorable for the development and spread of the pathogens. Monitoring data up to October 30th shows that in the first 10 months of 2026, the city recorded over 42,500 cases of dengue fever, including 11 deaths. Notably, the number of cases is trending upwards again, and the risk of further increases during the rainy season remains. The city also recorded over 44,000 cases of hand, foot, and mouth disease, an increase of over 65% compared to the same period last year, with 5 deaths and more than 1,600 outbreaks detected. In particular, the circulation of the EV71 virus strain, which can cause severe complications, raises concerns that this disease could erupt and spread widely in preschools . You may also like Ho Chi Minh City launches intensive campaign to prevent and control dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease. Monitoring data up to October 30th shows that in the first 10 months of 2026, the entire city recorded more than 42,500 cases of dengue fever, including 11 deaths. Ho Chi Minh City launches intensive campaign to prevent and control dengue fever and hand, foot and mouth disease. On the morning of October 31st, the Ho Chi Minh City Department of Health organized a rally to respond to the peak campaign for the prevention and control of Dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease in 2026. Dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease still lack specific treatments: What should people do? Ho Chi Minh City's health sector is stepping up efforts to prevent and control dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease, focusing on eliminating mosquito larvae, removing breeding grounds, and maintaining hygiene in schools to limit the risk of disease spread. "These figures serve as a clear warning; we must not be complacent, negligent, or slow in our actions. Every untreated mosquito breeding ground, every unsanitary living environment, every case of illness that goes undetected can increase the risk of community spread," Mr. Huynh Minh Chin emphasized. The Ho Chi Minh City Department of Health leadership requested that local authorities mobilize forces, organize comprehensive environmental sanitation, thoroughly review and address risk areas; strengthen inspection and supervision, and resolutely handle cases violating regulations on disease prevention and control, preventing the prolonged existence of risk areas that could lead to outbreaks. Health units should strengthen epidemiological surveillance, detect cases early, investigate and promptly handle outbreaks; and review and monitor dengue fever risk areas in their localities according to plan. Simultaneously, the health sector guides people to properly implement disease prevention measures; improve the capacity to detect warning signs, refer patients to appropriate facilities, and provide timely treatment, minimizing severe cases and deaths. The use of chemicals must be based on professional indications, targeted correctly, and according to proper procedures; indiscriminate spraying of chemicals or using it as a substitute for mosquito larva eradication is prohibited. For educational institutions, daycare centers, and childcare facilities, the Department of Health recommends strict daily health monitoring of children; early detection of suspected cases of hand, foot, and mouth disease, and notification to health facilities for guidance on handling. Maintain hygiene and disinfect classrooms, floors, desks, chairs, toys, and other items children frequently come into contact with. Strengthen coordination between the health sector, the education sector, and parents to ensure a safe learning environment and prevent the spread of disease in schools and the community. The Department of Health also urged city residents to actively implement disease prevention and control measures to protect themselves and their families. Each family should dedicate time at least once a week to inspect water containers, rinse and tightly cover water tanks and containers; remove bottles and waste materials that can collect water; eliminate mosquito larvae; and maintain hygiene in their living areas. In addition, people should develop the habit of washing their hands with soap under clean running water, especially before eating, before preparing food, and after caring for and cleaning children; and regularly clean toys, utensils, and frequently touched surfaces. You may also like Immediately after the launching ceremony, organizations, associations, and forces in Di An ward - a locality with a high number of dengue fever cases and one death - conducted on-site surveys of dengue fever risk areas and locations related to hand, foot, and mouth disease prevention and control, provided guidance on addressing shortcomings, and coordinated re-inspections as required. In parallel with key activities in Di An ward, the government and health sector of all wards, communes, and special zones of Ho Chi Minh City will also continue to promote activities in response to the campaign, such as organizing general environmental sanitation campaigns, inspecting and thoroughly handling high-risk areas and active outbreaks in the area. 胡志明市开展抗击登革热和手足口病运动 - SGGP英文版

HCMC launches campaign to combat dengue fever and hand, foot, mouth disease - SGGP English Edition
新闻简报据SGGP英文版报道,2026年10月31日,胡志明市卫生部发起了抗击登革热和手足口病的运动。截至上周,该市累计报告病例42500多例,其中死亡11例,仅上周报告疑似病例近1800例,较上月平均增加10%。与2025年同期相比,手足口病病例也增加了65%以上,死亡人数为5人。EV71 病毒目前正在传播,可导致幼儿出现严重并发症。卫生官员强调需要环境卫生、检查和社区参与来预防 暴发。部署了移动车辆和当地团队来提高认识并解决疾病热点问题。该活动旨在使疾病预防成为所有个人和家庭的常规做法,卫生部门敦促积极采取监测并提高监测能力,以尽量减少重症病例和死亡。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 10 月 31 日 09:26:48;2026 年 11 月 4 日 10:37:56;2026 年 11 月 5 日 00:49:03。
地点与对象
越南胡志明市;迪安区;学校和家庭。
事件
胡志明市卫生部启动2026年登革热和手足口病防控密集行动。SGGP英文版对此次活动进行了报道。
规模、影响与风险
As of the end of last week, the city had recorded more than 42,500 dengue fever cases, including 11 deaths. Nearly 1,800 suspected cases were reported last week alone, up almost 10 percent from the monthly average recorded in the previous month. More than 44,000 cases of hand, foot, and mouth disease were recorded, up over 65 percent from the same period in 2025, including five deaths. The EV71 virus is currently circulating and can cause severe complications in young children.
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生部门预测雨季期间病例可能会继续增加。全市各区、公社有关部门和卫生机构同时开展活动。流动宣传车沿着地安区的主要道路行驶,以提高公众意识。工作组走访当地,检查潜在热点地区,并就解决与 登革热 和手足口病相关的风险提供指导。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

HCMC launches campaign to combat dengue fever and hand, foot, mouth disease - SGGP English Edition

News Brief
On October 31, 2026, the Ho Chi Minh City Department of Health launched a campaign to combat dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease, as reported by SGGP English Edition. The city had recorded over 42,500 dengue fever cases, including 11 deaths, by the end of the previous week, with nearly 1,800 suspected cases reported in the last week alone, a 10% increase from the previous month's average. Hand, foot, and mouth disease cases also rose by over 65% compared to the same period in 2025, with five deaths. The EV71 virus, which can cause severe complications in young children, is currently circulating. Health officials emphasized the need for environmental sanitation, inspections, and community involvement to prevent outbreaks. Mobile vehicles and local teams were deployed to raise awareness and address disease hotspots. The campaign aims to make disease prevention a regular practice for all individuals and households, with the health sector urging proactive surveillance and improved treatment capacity to minimize severe cases and deaths.
Original source text (English)
November 5, 2026 at 00:49:03 SGGP Online SGGP Thể Thao SGGP Đầu tư Tài chính 中文版 SGGP Epaper SGGP English Edition Trang chủ National Ho Chi Minh City International Business Business Banking-finance Economy Self - introduction Stock market Highlights Livestock sector struggles ahead of Lunar New Year Gia Lai farmers rejoice as coffee harvest begins with favorable prices longform Latest 18 localities launch programs to help businesses access international markets November 4, 2026 at 10:37:56 AI-driven technologies create new value for businesses November 4, 2026 at 07:20:33 Five industrial expos open in Ho Chi Minh City with 800 exhibitors November 4, 2026 at 07:25:12 Health Education Sports Culture/art Law Science/technology Lifestyle Travel Weather Health HCMC launches campaign to combat dengue fever and hand, foot, mouth disease SGGPO October 31, 2026 at 09:26:48 On the morning of October 31, the Ho Chi Minh City Department of Health held a rally to launch the 2026 intensive campaign to prevent and control dengue fever and hand, foot, and mouth disease. Health officials and local authorities inspect potential dengue fever hotspots and provide guidance on preventive measures in Di An Ward. (Photo: SGGP) According to the Ho Chi Minh City Center for Disease Control (HCDC), as of the end of last week, the city had recorded more than 42,500 dengue fever cases, including 11 deaths. Nearly 1,800 suspected cases were reported last week alone, up almost 10 percent from the monthly average recorded in the previous month. The health sector forecasts that cases could continue to rise during the rainy season, when weather conditions favor the breeding of Aedes mosquitoes, which transmit dengue fever. Regarding hand, foot, and mouth disease, the city has recorded more than 44,000 cases, up over 65 percent from the same period in 2025, including five deaths. More than 1,600 outbreaks have been detected, most of them in schools. Notably, the EV71 virus is currently circulating and can cause severe complications in young children. Speaking at the campaign launch, Dr. Huynh Minh Chin, Deputy Director of the Ho Chi Minh City Department of Health, called on local authorities to mobilize forces for simultaneous environmental sanitation drives, identify and thoroughly address potential disease hotspots, and step up inspections and surveillance to prevent outbreaks from persisting in communities. The health sector must proactively strengthen epidemiological surveillance, detect cases early, promptly contain outbreaks, and improve treatment capacity to minimize severe cases and deaths, he said. Educational institutions and childcare groups were urged to regularly monitor children's health, clean and disinfect classrooms and toys, and closely coordinate with healthcare facilities whenever suspected cases of hand, foot and mouth disease are detected. Everyone should actively participate in disease prevention and control through practical actions. Every household should set aside time at least once a week to inspect and remove containers holding stagnant water, eliminate mosquito larvae, and maintain environmental hygiene. Dr. Huynh Minh Chin, Deputy Director of the Ho Chi Minh City Department of Health, speaks at the event. (Photo: SGGP) To prevent hand, foot, and mouth disease, people should maintain the habit of washing their hands with soap, cleaning toys and household items, and taking children to healthcare facilities if they show suspected symptoms, Dr. Huynh Minh Chin urged. According to Dr. Huynh Minh Chin, disease prevention and control is not solely the responsibility of the health sector but a shared responsibility of society as a whole. It should not be limited to the days of the campaign launch but become a voluntary and regular practice for every individual and household. Every small action taken today will help prevent disease risks tomorrow. We must remain determined to ensure that every neighborhood, residential area, school, and household becomes an active participant in disease prevention; every identified risk is addressed promptly; and every resident has access to accurate information and knows how to protect their own health, as well as that of their family and community, Dr. Huynh Minh Chin said. Immediately after the launch ceremony, five mobile public information vehicles traveled along major roads in Di An Ward to raise public awareness. Working teams also visited local areas to inspect potential hotspots and provide guidance on addressing risks associated with dengue fever and hand, foot and mouth disease. Authorities and health agencies in wards and communes across the city simultaneously launched activities in response to the campaign. 重症登革热 Surge:管理休克和重复病例 - OC Academy

Severe Dengue Surge: Managing Shock and Repeat Cases - OC Academy
新闻简报文章讨论了孟加拉国 重症登革热 病例激增的情况,强调了继发感染和休克的危险。最近的流行病学报告表明,由于同时循环的登革热血清型引发了严重的血管通透性,导致住院和死亡人数急剧增加。南亚的临床医生应在季节转换期间保持警惕,因为气候条件变化导致 传播 窗口期延长,导致患者持续涌入。本文概述了治疗 重症登革热 的临床方案,包括及时液体复苏、生命体征监测以及仔细滴定静脉液体以避免肺水肿等并发症。它还解释了继发感染的病理生理学,其中非中和抗体增强病毒进入 宿主 细胞,导致快速复制和强烈的炎症级联反应。这些因素会导致严重的血管渗漏和休克,因此需要在无发热期间进行密切监测。该文章可作为医疗保健专业人员改善对登革热 暴发 的反应的指南,强调更新培训和方案的重要性。

孟加拉国和南亚。文章提到了对该地区医疗保健系统和临床医生的影响,特别是在季节性转变期间。
