Severe Dengue Surge: Managing Shock and Repeat Cases - OC Academy

News Brief
The article discusses a severe dengue surge in Bangladesh, highlighting the dangers of secondary infections and shock. Recent epidemiological reports indicate an alarming increase in hospitalizations and fatalities due to co-circulating dengue serotypes, which trigger profound vascular permeability. Clinicians in South Asia are urged to remain vigilant during seasonal transitions, as the extended transmission window caused by changing climatic conditions leads to sustained patient influxes. The article outlines clinical protocols for managing severe dengue, including timely fluid resuscitation, monitoring of vital signs, and careful titration of intravenous fluids to avoid complications like pulmonary edema. It also explains the pathophysiology of secondary infections, where non-neutralizing antibodies enhance viral entry into host cells, leading to rapid replication and an intense inflammatory cascade. These factors contribute to severe vascular leakage and shock, necessitating close monitoring during the afebrile period. The piece serves as a guide for healthcare professionals to improve their response to dengue outbreaks, emphasizing the importance of updated training and protocols.
Original source text (English)
Clinical ProgramsSpecialist ProgramsCertificationsExplore Login / Signup Login / Signup Clinical ProgramsSpecialist ProgramsShort Term CertificationsSpecialitiesNeet PG Mock TestRefer & EarnBlogsContact UsFAQs Healthcare BlogsMedical UpdatesSevere Dengue Surge: Managing Shock and Repeat Cases Medical Updates Severe Dengue Surge: Managing Shock and Repeat Cases Published on Nov 2, 2026 3 min read "Rising dengue cases highlight the danger of secondary infections and shock. Learn the clinical protocols for severe dengue management and critical triage." Recent epidemiological reports from Bangladesh reveal an alarming escalation in hospitalizations and fatal complications. Therefore, regional clinicians must prioritize rigorous severe dengue management during seasonal transitions. Health authorities recorded hundreds of fatalities and overflowing emergency wards as co-circulating serotypes triggered profound vascular permeability. Furthermore, secondary infections consistently provoke life-threatening hemodynamic instability. Clinicians across South Asia must remain alert to rapid clinical deterioration during the critical defervescent phase. Pathophysiology of Repeat Infections and Severe Disease Secondary dengue infections pose significant diagnostic and clinical hurdles. When a patient contracts a different viral serotype, preexisting antibodies bind to the new pathogen without neutralizing it. Consequently, this phenomenon, known as antibody-dependent enhancement, accelerates viral entry into host monocytes. As a result, massive viral replication triggers a sudden cytokine cascade. In addition, this inflammatory storm disrupts endothelial junction integrity and promotes plasma leakage. Patients frequently develop pleural effusions, ascites, and hemoconcentration. Thus, early recognition of capillary leakage prevents uncompensated hypovolemic collapse. Clinical Protocols for Severe Dengue Management Timely fluid resuscitation forms the foundation of effective patient care. Therefore, physicians should immediately identify warning signs, such as persistent vomiting, mucosal bleeding, and severe abdominal pain. When hematocrit levels rise sharply alongside dropping platelet counts, clinicians must initiate isotonic crystalloid infusion without hesitation. However, medical teams must titrate intravenous fluids carefully to avoid pulmonary edema. In addition, healthcare professionals should regularly record blood pressure, pulse pressure, and urine output. Narrowing pulse pressure below 20 mmHg signals impending decompensated shock. Consequently, close monitoring during the afebrile period saves vulnerable lives, an essential competency emphasized when working in emergency medicine. Environmental Shifts and Extended Transmission Windows Changing climatic conditions are actively altering vector dynamics across South Asia. Specifically, erratic monsoon patterns and persistent post-monsoon humidity create enduring breeding reservoirs for Aedes mosquitoes. Entomologists observe that warmer temperatures accelerate viral incubation periods within the vector. Because transmission seasons now extend into late winter months, outpatient departments face sustained patient influxes. Furthermore, urban density and inadequate vector source reduction facilitate relentless co-circulation of multiple DENV serotypes. Clinicians must maintain heightened clinical vigilance even as traditional monsoon months conclude, backed by up-to-date training available through various general practice speciality courses. Frequently Asked Questions Q1: Why do secondary dengue infections carry a higher risk of shock? Secondary infections often trigger antibody-dependent enhancement. In this process, non-neutralizing cross-reactive antibodies from an earlier infection assist the entry of a different serotype into immune cells. As a result, viral replication accelerates, prompting an intense inflammatory cascade that causes severe vascular leakage and shock. Q2: What clinical indicators indicate the onset of severe dengue? Key warning signs include severe abdominal pain, persistent vomiting, mucosal bleeding, lethargy, and clinical fluid accumulation. Furthermore, a concurrent rise in hematocrit and a rapid decline in platelet counts strongly indicate active plasma leakage requiring immediate hospital care. Q3: How should clinicians approach fluid replacement in dengue shock? Physicians should administer isotonic crystalloids in carefully titrated boluses while continuously monitoring vital parameters and urine output. However, clinicians must avoid excessive fluid loading, which can precipitate pulmonary congestion and cardiac compromise during the reabsorption phase. MND 瓜达拉哈拉当地:水危机如何影响当地企业、登革热病例减少以及新的“哈利斯科制造”印章 - 墨西哥新闻日报

MND Local Guadalajara: How the water crisis is affecting local businesses, fewer dengue cases and a new 'Made in Jalisco' seal - Mexico News Daily
新闻简报墨西哥新闻日报 2026 年 11 月 2 日发表的文章讨论了哈利斯科州瓜达拉哈拉持续的水危机对当地企业的影响以及登革热病例的大幅下降。截至 2026 年 10 月 11 日,哈利斯科州报告了 242 例登革热确诊病例,比 2025 年下降了 90%,其中 2 例确诊死亡,另有 5 例正在接受调查。由于家庭将水存放在未加盖的容器中,水危机导致登革热风险增加,促使卫生官员建议盖上这些容器以防止蚊子繁殖。目前正在传播毒性最强的登革热血清型,血清型 2,可引起登革出血热。尽管发生了危机,哈利斯科州州长还是强调了该州强劲的外国直接投资,尽管一些经济干扰是显而易见的。企业正在通过使用过滤系统和私人供水来适应。文章还提到推出新的“哈利斯科制造”印章以推广当地产品,但没有具体说明其与预防登革热的直接联系。预计水危机将持续下去,官员们计划关闭部分系统并定量供水,直到更换系统建成为止。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章于 2026 年 11 月 2 日发表。瓜达拉哈拉的水危机仍在持续,据报道,截至 2026 年 10 月 11 日登革热病例有所下降。
地点与对象
墨西哥哈利斯科州的瓜达拉哈拉都会区 (GMA),当地企业、餐馆、酒店、医院和制造商受到影响。文章还提到了科库拉、特乌奇特兰等高风险地区以及巴亚尔塔港等沿海地区。
事件
哈利斯科州的登革热病例显着减少,与 2025 年相比,截至 2026 年 10 月 11 日确诊病例为 242 例。瓜达拉哈拉的水危机导致由于未加盖的容器中储水而导致登革热风险增加,促使卫生当局建议覆盖这些容器以防止蚊子繁殖。
规模、影响与风险
哈利斯科州的登革热病例同比下降 90%,其中两人确诊死亡,另有 5 人正在接受调查。水危机造成了经济混乱,企业通过使用过滤系统和私人供水来适应。毒性最强的血清型(血清型 2)正在流行,可导致登革出血热。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生当局重申了盖住储水容器以防止蚊子繁殖的重要性。哈利斯科州政府正在通过关闭部分系统并实行配水来解决水危机,直到更换系统建成为止。新的“哈利斯科制造”标志的推出是为了推广当地产品,但其对预防登革热的影响并未明确说明。

MND Local Guadalajara: How the water crisis is affecting local businesses, fewer dengue cases and a new 'Made in Jalisco' seal - Mexico News Daily

News Brief
The article from Mexico News Daily, published on November 2, 2026, discusses the impact of the ongoing water crisis in Guadalajara, Jalisco, on local businesses and the significant decline in dengue cases. Through October 11, 2026, Jalisco reported 242 confirmed dengue cases, a 90% drop compared to 2025, with two confirmed deaths and five more under investigation. The water crisis has led to increased dengue risk as families store water in uncovered containers, prompting health officials to advise covering these to prevent mosquito breeding. The most virulent dengue serotype, serotype 2, is currently circulating, which can cause dengue hemorrhagic fever. Despite the crisis, the Jalisco governor highlighted strong foreign direct investment in the state, though some economic disruption is evident. Businesses are adapting by using filtration systems and private water deliveries. The article also mentions the launch of a new 'Made in Jalisco' seal to promote local products, but its direct link to dengue prevention is not specified. The water crisis is expected to continue, with officials planning to shut down parts of the system and ration water until replacements are built.

Home El Bajío El Bajío News MND Local Guadalajara: How the water crisis is affecting local businesses, fewer dengue cases and a new ‘Made in Jalisco’ seal By Dawn Stoner November 2, 2026 0 Dengue cases are down significantly in Jalisco, but Guadalajara's ongoing water crisis continues to prompt warnings. (Kenneth Lopez/Unsplash) This past week, business leaders and government officials sparred over whether the ongoing water crisis in the Guadalajara Metro Area (GMA) will affect business investment in Jalisco, so we looked at the facts. Meanwhile, as the rainy season winds down, dengue cases have fallen sharply across the state compared to 2025, and a new “Hecho en Jalisco” (“Made in Jalisco”) seal aims to boost the visibility and cachet of select local products. Business leader warns investment will suffer due to water quality issues José Medina Mora, president of the Business Coordinating Council, believes the ongoing water crisis in Guadalajara will slow investment in Jalisco. (Graciela López/Cuartoscuro) Speaking to local journalists on Oct. 23, José Medina Mora, president of the Business Coordinating Council (CCE), issued a stark warning to Jalisco state officials. Medina Mora said he expects investment projects in Jalisco to be negatively impacted by Guadalajara’s ongoing water crisis, noting, “There will be investment decisions in Jalisco, and in Guadalajara particularly, that will be halted until we can offer quality water.” Medina Mora also blamed local politicians for their failure to anticipate and solve these issues before they became acute. Jalisco governor details US $1.3B investment in Guadalajara’s water infrastructure: Wednesday’s mañanera recapped Later clarifying his comments, Medina Mora said the CCE does not expect the water crisis to spark capital flight from Jalisco but expects new investments to be put on hold due to the increased business risk posed by water contamination. State and federal government officials swiftly pushed back on Medina Mora’s comments. Government points to strong FDI as local businesses adapt Ximena Escobedo Juárez, undersecretary of industry at the Ministry of Industry and Commerce of the Ministry of Economy, cited strong business investment across Jalisco in 2026 as evidence that the business community remains undeterred. The ongoing water crisis in the GMA has reached the point where officials will be shutting down the parts of the system most responsible for the dirty water and rationing the reduced supply until replacements are built and operating. (Fernando Carranza García / Cuartoscuro.com) In the first half of 2026, Jalisco attracted US $1.41 billion in new capital, surpassing the US $1.2 billion invested in the state for all of 2025. This contrasts with a sharp decline in foreign direct investment in Mexico’s first half overall. Yet some economic disruption is undeniable. With municipal water supplies being rationed over the next six months, many local businesses are scrambling to arrange deliveries from private water trucks to avoid major disruptions to area restaurants, hotels, hospitals and other local service providers. In addition, manufacturers around Guadalajara have reportedly been supplementing operations with more sophisticated filtration protocols, including on-site chlorination systems, sediment micro-filters and reverse osmosis units. These enhancements are needed to remove heavy metals, sediment and fecal matter from the municipal water that enters their facilities and keep contaminated water from damaging their industrial equipment. Rainy season ebbs with dengue cases in Jalisco down by 90% Through Oct. 11, Jalisco has 242 confirmed dengue cases in 2026, ranking 12th nationwide . This represents a massive improvement over 2025, when Jalisco ranked third nationwide for dengue, behind Sonora and Veracruz. Year to date, there have been two confirmed deaths, with five more under investigation. Last year, the state recorded 10 dengue deaths. Dengue cases in Jalisco Year Confirmed Cases Percent change, year over year 2023 941 N/A 2024 20,907 2,122% 2025 2,317 -89% 2026 (through Oct. 11) 242 -90% Source: Secretaría de Salud Some strains of dengue are more virulent than others. One of the serotypes that is spreading in Jalisco right now is the most virulent form (serotype 2), which causes dengue hemorrhagic fever. Year to date, both serotypes 2 and 3 of the virus are circulating in Jalisco. It is a mistake to think that most people get infected with dengue in remote, jungle areas. Rapid urbanization and increased population density have created conditions ideal for mosquito breeding and infection. Yordi Raúl Portillo Montes, who heads Jalisco’s National Public Health Service, noted that the highest incidence of dengue in the state has occurred in Cocula and Teuchitlán, with coastal areas, including Puerto Vallarta, also at high risk. In the GMA, central Guadalajara, Tonalá and Tlaquepaque have recorded the most cases this year. While dengue is less common outside of the rainy season, the risk does not fall to zero. Due to the water crisis affecting Guadalajara, hundreds of families have resorted to storing water in buckets, drums or other containers, which has increased dengue risk in urban areas. In response, health authorities have reiterated the importance of covering these containers to discourage mosquito breeding. While there is a vaccine for dengue, it can be difficult to access in Mexico, so your best bet is to be proactive and avoid infection. The new ‘Made in Jalisco’ seal aims to promote and protect local products View this post on Instagram A post shared by Secretaría de Desarrollo Económico Jalisco (@sedeco.jalisco) In a bid to strengthen the marketing of products made in the state, the Jalisco Ministry of Economic Development (Sedeco) recently announced the launch of the “Made in Jalisco” seal . The initiative aims to increase visibility for Jalisco-made products with protected designations in foreign markets while ensuring that the economic benefits flow through to local communities where they are made. The new seal is expected to recognize at least 40 businesses that make products with a designation of origin or geographical indication. Participation will be free, and eligible businesses include companies, cooperatives, workshops and individual producers of tequila, raicilla, Yahualica chili or Sayula cajeta that have authorization from the Mexican Institute of Industrial Property (IMPI). Agave: The noble plant at the heart of Mexican culture However, the Jalisco Ministry of Economic Development (Sedeco) and the civil association El Telar will decide which products are eligible to display the new seal. Applicants will be judged on their history and roots in Jalisco, their products’ distinctive qualities, organizational preparedness and desire for designation of origin protection. According to state figures, the tequila supply chain supports more than 100,000 families, while raicilla involves more than 80 artisanal producers. Yahualica chili peppers are cultivated by roughly 250 small farmers and collectives, and Sayula cajeta is produced in 21 family workshops. MND writer Dawn Stoner is reporting from Guadalajara. 孟加拉国登革热疫情恶化:418人死亡 - Zamon.uz

Dengue epidemic worsens in Bangladesh: 418 people have died - Zamon.uz
新闻简报孟加拉国正在经历 重症登革热 热病流行,自今年年初以来已有 418 人死亡。仅 10 月份就有 173 人死亡,超过 9 月份的 148 人死亡,创下 2026 年迄今为止的最高月度死亡人数。已有超过 133,000 名患者住院治疗,预计情况将持续到 11 月份,直到 12 月份才可能出现明显改善。卫生部尚未推出登革热 疫苗,但官员们正在考虑将其用作长期战略。专家将正在进行的传播归因于环境因素,包括之前的降雨创造了有利的蚊子繁殖地,以及气候变化延长了繁殖季节。这种流行病给孟加拉国的医疗保健系统带来了额外的压力,该系统也在应对大规模麻疹暴发。医生警告说,许多患者只是在出现严重并发症后才寻求 治疗,这强调了早期干预的必要性。

时间

孟加拉国的 登革热 疫情正在恶化,自今年年初以来已有 418 人死亡,超过 133,000 人住院治疗。专家预计,疫情将持续到11月,直到12月才可能出现明显好转。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dengue epidemic worsens in Bangladesh: 418 people have died - Zamon.uz

News Brief
Bangladesh is experiencing a severe dengue fever epidemic, with 418 deaths reported since the start of the year. In October alone, 173 people died, surpassing the 148 deaths in September, marking the highest monthly toll so far in 2026. Over 133,000 patients have been hospitalized, and the situation is expected to persist through November, with significant improvement unlikely until December. The Ministry of Health has not yet introduced a dengue vaccine, but officials are considering its use as a long-term strategy. Experts attribute the ongoing transmission to environmental factors, including previous rainfall creating favorable mosquito breeding sites and climate change extending the breeding season. The epidemic is placing additional pressure on Bangladesh's healthcare system, which is also dealing with a large measles outbreak. Doctors warn that many patients seek treatment only after severe complications arise, highlighting the need for early intervention.
Original source text (English)
Uzbekistan World Politics Crime Culture Sport Health Lifestyle ENG +12°C 1 USD = 11,975 World 21:57 / 31.10.2026 Share Dengue epidemic worsens in Bangladesh: 418 people have died Experts forecast that the epidemic will continue in November as well, and the situation will not improve even until December. Bangladesh is experiencing a worsening dengue fever epidemic. Since the beginning of the year, 418 people have died from the disease, and more than 133,000 patients have been hospitalized. Experts report that virus transmission may continue through November, with a significant decline unlikely to be observed until December. According to the country's Ministry of Health, 173 people died from dengue fever in October. This is more than the 148 deaths recorded in September, making it the month with the highest death toll so far in 2026. At the same time, the country is also battling the world's largest measles epidemic. This disease has claimed the lives of more than 1,200 people. The simultaneous spread of two epidemics is placing an additional burden on Bangladesh's healthcare system. Doctors say that many patients seek hospital care only after the disease has begun to cause severe complications. At Shahid Suhrawardy Medical College and Hospital in Dhaka, 279 patients with dengue fever are being treated. Another 67 people were admitted to the facility in a single day. Experts emphasize that reinfection, as well as delays in diagnosis and treatment, are increasing the risk of severe cases. Because all four serotypes of the dengue virus are circulating in the country, a person previously infected can be reinfected with a different serotype and face severe complications. According to the forecast of Jahangirnagar University entomologist Kabirul Bashar, the incidence may decrease somewhat in November, but virus transmission will continue. In his view, there is a high probability that significant improvement will not be observed until December. The expert explained this by the fact that previous rainfall has created favorable breeding sites for mosquitoes. In addition, changes in rainfall and humidity patterns due to climate change are extending the mosquito breeding season. Fourteen of Carles’ 33 barangays have reported dengue clustering, the most of any local government unit (LGU) in Iloilo province. The northern Iloilo town recorded 406 dengue cases and five deaths from Jan. 4 to Oct. 24, according to data from the Iloilo Provi - Facebook

News Brief
The news report highlights that fourteen barangays in Carles, a town in Iloilo province, have reported dengue clustering, the highest number among any local government unit (LGU) in the province. According to data from the Iloilo Provincial Health Office–Provincial Epidemiology and Surveillance Unit (IPHO-PESU), covering Morbidity Week 42, the town recorded 406 dengue cases and five deaths from January 4 to October 24, 2026. The report was published on November 4, 2026, by the Daily Guardian on Facebook, and the full article is available in the comment section. This information indicates a significant outbreak in Carles, with a notable impact on public health. The report does not provide further details on the specific dengue virus strains or the response measures taken by local authorities.

Other regions such as Barisal, Rajshahi, Mymensingh, Rangpur, and Sylhet also experienced cases, showing the disease's spread across the country. The report confirms these figures but does not specify the exact risk level or detailed response measures. The country continues to face the spread of the disease after recording the highest monthly death toll in October. According to data from the "Directorate General of Health Services" reported by "BD News 24" on Sunday, the total number of hospital admissions due to the disease reached 136,929 cases since the beginning of the year, with 130,388 patients discharged after receiving treatment. The data indicated that October recorded 174 deaths from dengue fever, making it the month with the highest deaths since the beginning of the year, compared to 148 deaths in September. The "Daily Star" reported, based on official health data, that 6,114 patients are still receiving treatment in hospitals across the country. Dhaka Division recorded the highest number of new hospital admissions, with 831 cases from Saturday morning to Sunday morning, followed by Khulna Division with 259 cases, and Chittagong with 257 cases. Other cases were distributed across Barisal Division with 213 cases, Rajshahi with 185 cases, Mymensingh with 92 cases, Rangpur with 73 cases, and Sylhet with 19 cases, reflecting the spread of the disease in various regions of the country. The official figures refer to hospital admissions and do not necessarily include all dengue fever cases, as some infections may go unnoticed or present mild symptoms not requiring hospitalization. Bangladesh's health authorities have been maintaining records of admissions and deaths related to dengue fever since 2000. The country experienced its worst recorded outbreak of the disease in 2023, when the number of hospital admissions reached 321,179, with 1,705 deaths, according to data from "BD News 24". Despite the rise in deaths last October, the recorded toll since the beginning of 2026 remains lower than the figures during the outbreak in 2023. Dengue fever is a viral disease primarily transmitted to humans through the bites of infected Aedes mosquitoes, and it particularly spreads in tropical and subtropical regions. The "World Health Organization" estimates that about half of the world's population is at risk of contracting the disease, with an estimated 100 million to 400 million infections occurring annually worldwide. The organization clarifies that most infections do not cause symptoms or result in mild symptoms, although some cases may develop into severe disease that poses life-threatening risks. Common symptoms include fever, headache, body aches, nausea, and rash, while signs of severe infection may appear after fever subsides. Key danger signs include severe abdominal pain, persistent vomiting, rapid breathing, and bleeding, which are symptoms requiring urgent medical care. The "World Health Organization" emphasizes that early diagnosis and provision of proper medical care contribute to reducing deaths due to severe cases, even though there is no specific treatment that eliminates the virus itself. According to the "Centers for Disease Control and Prevention", four closely related viral serotypes can cause dengue fever, meaning that recovery from a previous infection does not provide complete protection against subsequent infections. Second locally acquired dengue case confirmed in Manatee County - ABC7 WWSB

News Brief
On October 30, 2026, the Florida Department of Health in Manatee County confirmed a second locally acquired dengue case, following the first case reported by WWSB a week earlier. The health department has not disclosed the patients' conditions, the connection between the cases, or the specific dates and locations of mosquito-control treatments. Officials are working with county departments and Manatee County Mosquito Control to enhance surveillance and prevention efforts, including truck fogging, aerial larviciding, and localized inspections for mosquito breeding areas. However, the exact exposure locations remain unknown. Dengue fever is spread through the bite of an infected Aedes aegypti mosquito and can cause symptoms such as fever, severe muscle and joint pain, and sometimes a rash. Health officials are urging residents to take preventive measures to reduce mosquito bites and breeding areas around their homes.

(WWSB) - Health officials have confirmed a second locally acquired dengue case in Manatee County. The Florida Department of Health in Manatee County announced the update Friday, one week after WWSB reported the county’s first publicly announced locally acquired dengue case. DOH-Manatee said it is working with county departments and Manatee County Mosquito Control on increased surveillance and prevention efforts. The work includes truck fogging, aerial larviciding and localized inspections for mosquito breeding areas. The department did not identify where either person may have been exposed. It also did not release the patients’ conditions, say whether the cases are connected or provide dates and locations for mosquito-control treatments. Dengue fever case confirmed in Manatee County as cases rise across Florida Manatee County confirms locally acquired dengue case Dengue is spread through the bite of an infected Aedes aegypti mosquito, according to the health department. It is not spread person-to-person. Symptoms can include fever, severe muscle and joint pain, and sometimes a rash. The department says symptoms can appear within 15 days after a bite from an infected mosquito. Health officials are asking residents to reduce mosquito bites and breeding areas around their homes. Recommended steps include wearing loose-fitting long sleeves and pants, applying EPA-registered insect repellent, repairing damaged window and door screens, and draining standing water from containers such as buckets, pool covers, coolers, toys and flowerpots. Anyone with symptoms should contact a health care provider, according to DOH-Manatee. Copyright 2026 WWSB. All rights reserved. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Manatee confirms second local dengue case amid Florida surge - Sarasota Herald-Tribune

News Brief
Manatee County, Florida, confirmed its second locally acquired case of dengue on October 30, 2026, at 12:31 p.m. ET, amid a surge in dengue infections across the state, which has reported 485 cases. The Florida Department of Health stated that health officials are collaborating with local departments and mosquito control to enhance surveillance and prevention measures. These efforts include increased truck fogging, aerial larvicide treatments, and ground inspections to identify mosquito breeding sites. Officials are also urging residents to take precautions such as wearing long-sleeved clothing, using EPA-registered insect repellents, and eliminating standing water around homes to reduce mosquito populations. Dengue is transmitted through the bite of an infected Aedes aegypti mosquito and is not contagious from person to person. Symptoms can include fever, severe muscle and joint pain, and a rash, typically appearing within 15 days of a bite. Residents are advised to contact healthcare providers if they experience symptoms of dengue.
Original source text (English)
News Sports Ticket Lifestyle Real Estate Advertise Obituaries eNewspaper Legals SARASOTA Manatee confirms second local dengue case amid Florida surge Manatee County confirms its second locally acquired dengue case as Florida infections surge. Wade Tatangelo Sarasota Herald-Tribune Oct. 30, 2026, 12:31 p.m. ET Manatee County has confirmed its second locally acquired case of dengue as Florida experiences a surge in the mosquito-borne illness. The Florida Department of Health in Manatee County announced the new case Oct. 30, saying health officials have been working with Manatee County departments and Manatee County Mosquito Control on increased surveillance and prevention efforts. Those efforts include increased truck fogging, aerial larvicide treatments and ground inspections for locations where mosquitoes could be breeding. The Manatee County case comes amid a sharp rise in dengue infections across Florida. The state has reported 485 dengue cases, according to previously published USA TODAY Network - Florida reporting. Dengue is transmitted through the bite of an infected Aedes aegypti mosquito. The disease is not contagious from person to person. Symptoms can resemble the flu and include fever, severe muscle and joint pain and, in some cases, a rash. Respiratory symptoms generally are not associated with dengue. Symptoms can appear within 15 days after a person is bitten by an infected mosquito, according to the Florida Department of Health. Health officials are urging Manatee County residents to take precautions to avoid mosquito bites. Residents should wear loose-fitting, long-sleeved shirts and pants and use insect repellents registered by the U.S. Environmental Protection Agency on exposed skin and clothing. EPA-registered repellents are considered safe and effective when used as directed, including for pregnant and breastfeeding women, according to health officials. Residents also should eliminate standing water around their homes that can provide mosquito breeding grounds. Officials recommend draining water from garbage cans, buckets, pool covers, coolers, toys, flowerpots and other containers where rainwater or sprinkler water can collect. Damaged screens on windows, doors, porches and patios also should be repaired to help prevent mosquitoes from getting inside. The Florida Department of Health conducts statewide surveillance for mosquito-borne illnesses, including dengue, West Nile virus, Eastern equine encephalitis, St. Louis encephalitis, malaria and chikungunya. Anyone experiencing symptoms of dengue should contact a health care provider. Wade Tatangelo is the executive editor of the Sarasota Herald-Tribune and Florida dining and entertainment editor for the USA TODAY Network. Follow him on Facebook, Instagram, and X. 能够传播 登革热 的恶性“咬脚踝”蚊子入侵加州沿海城市 - 纽约邮报

Vicious ‘ankle biter’ mosquitoes capable of spreading dengue fever invade California coastal city - New York Post
新闻简报该文章报道了加利福尼亚州圣巴巴拉县存在“咬脚踝”蚊子,包括“埃及伊蚊”和“诺托伊蚊”。这些蚊子以白天叮咬脚和脚踝周围而闻名,能够传播 登革热。圣巴巴拉县蚊子和 媒介 管理区证实,2026 年 10 月在特定地点附近发现了 4 只“埃及伊蚊”。文章还提到洛杉矶县存在西尼罗河病毒,今年报告了 180 多例病例,奥兰治县设置了 850 个西尼罗河病毒阳性蚊子捕捉器。然而,所提供的证据中没有报告确诊的登革热病例或死亡病例。鼓励居民向该机构报告任何遇到攻击性蚊子的情况。文章强调,由于创纪录的热浪和厄尔尼诺现象的威胁,蚊子活动增加,这创造了有利的繁殖条件。消息来源是一家知名媒体,该信息基于当地卫生部门的官方报告。
查看新闻五要素
时间
The article was published on 2026-11-03. The mosquitoes were found in October 2026, with specific dates mentioned as Oct. 20, and earlier sightings in March and June 2026.
地点与对象
这些蚊子是在加利福尼亚州圣巴巴拉县发现的,特别是在北沃伦塔里奥、东科塔、拉古纳、东卡里洛、东维多利亚和奥利弗街等社区。文章还提到奥兰治县和洛杉矶县是与蚊子活动相关的地区。
事件
恶性“咬脚踝”蚊子,特别是“埃及伊蚊”和“伊蚊”,已经入侵南加州沿海地区。这些蚊子能够传播 登革热,并以白天叮咬脚和脚踝周围而闻名。
规模、影响与风险
圣巴巴拉县蚊子和 媒介 管理区证实存在四只“埃及伊蚊”。文章还指出,今年洛杉矶县的西尼罗河病毒病例已飙升至 180 多例,奥兰治县已设置了 850 个西尼罗河病毒阳性的陷阱。然而,所提供的证据中没有报告确诊的登革热病例或死亡病例。
应对、状态与不确定性
如果居民白天在室内或室外遇到攻击性蚊子,请联系该地区。该机构正在开展蚊子诱捕和监测活动。该文章没有提供有关调查当前状态或与 登革病毒 传播 相关的任何已确认风险评估的详细信息。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Vicious ‘ankle biter’ mosquitoes capable of spreading dengue fever invade California coastal city - New York Post

News Brief
The article reports the presence of 'ankle biter' mosquitoes, including 'Aedes aegypti' and 'Aedes notoscriptus,' in Santa Barbara County, California. These mosquitoes, known for biting around the feet and ankles during the day, are capable of spreading dengue fever. Santa Barbara County’s Mosquito and Vector Management District confirmed the discovery of four 'Aedes aegypti' mosquitoes near specific locations in October 2026. The article also mentions the presence of West Nile virus in Los Angeles County, with over 180 cases reported this year, and Orange County pulling 850 traps with West Nile-positive mosquitoes. However, no confirmed dengue cases or deaths are reported in the supplied evidence. Residents are encouraged to report any encounters with aggressive mosquitoes to the agency. The article highlights the increased mosquito activity due to record heat waves and El Niño threats, which have created favorable breeding conditions. The source is a reputable media outlet, and the information is based on official reports from local health authorities.

Got it Edition CA NY US News Metro Long Island Politics World News Search Search Recommended Skip to main content California News Vicious ‘ankle biter’ mosquitoes capable of spreading dengue fever invade California coastal city By Sheetal Banchariya Published Nov. 3, 2026, 3:43 p.m. ET 55 Comments See more of our coverage in your search results. Make California Post a Preferred Source 1/1 Skip Ad Continue watchingafter the adVisit Advertiser websiteGO TO PAGE These mosquitoes don’t wait for dusk and go straight for your ankles! Yellow fever mosquitoes native to Sub-Saharan Africa have been wreaking havoc in several neighborhoods of picturesque coastal areas in Southern California. Santa Barbara County’s Mosquito and Vector Management District confirmed that four ‘Aedes aegypti’ blood-suckers were found on Oct. 30 near North Voluntario and East Cota streets. Residents tormented by the mosquitoes have named them “ankle biters.” SILVIA MAQQ – stock.adobe.com The agency said a total of four ‘Aedes aegypti’ mosquitoes, capable of causing dengue fever, were trapped in different locations across Santa Barbara last week. Two were found near North Voluntario and Cota streets, one at Laguna and East Carrillo, and one near East Victoria and Olive. According to the District, the same species was found Oct. 20 in the eastern Goleta Valley, near Turnpike Road and Calle Real, and in Carpinteria in July. A second non-native species is already in town. Australian backyard mosquitoes, ‘Aedes notoscriptus,’ were found in Santa Barbara’s West Side in March and in the Mesa neighborhood in June. Both species like to bite around the feet and ankles, and they do it during the day, indoors and out. Residents tormented by the pests have named them “ankle biters.” “Please contact the District if you find yourself being bitten by aggressive mosquitoes indoors or outdoors during daytime hours,” the agency said, asking residents to call 805-969-5050 or send a message through the “Contact Us” page on its website. Dengue fever is often accompanied by severe body aches, giving it the nickname “breakbone fever.” According to the Mayo Clinic, individuals typically display symptoms 4 to 10 days after being bitten by an infected mosquito Australian backyard mosquitoes, ‘Aedes notoscriptus,’ were found in Santa Barbara’s West Side in March. Getty Images Santa Barbara’s troubles come as the whole region swelters through what many residents call a year of agony. Record-shattering heat waves and El Niño threats have turned Southern California into a breeding paradise for the winged pests. “I’ve been here my whole life and the amount of mosquitoes I’ve seen this summer has been insane,” one woman said in a TikTok video that has drawn more than 21,000 likes. 5 What do you think? Post a comment. Los Angeles County has recorded over 180 cases of West Nile virus this year—the highest spike in nearly a decade, according to the local public health department. The region’s native ‘Culex’ mosquito picks up West Nile from wild birds before passing it on to humans. In nearby Orange County, vector control officials have pulled a staggering 850 traps loaded with West Nile-positive bugs, reports the Guardian. Time
The article was published on 2026-11-03. The mosquitoes were found in October 2026, with specific dates mentioned as Oct. 20, and earlier sightings in March and June 2026.
Location and population
The mosquitoes were found in Santa Barbara County, California, specifically in neighborhoods like North Voluntario, East Cota, Laguna, East Carrillo, East Victoria, and Olive streets. The article also mentions Orange County and Los Angeles County as areas with related mosquito activity.
Event
Vicious ‘ankle biter’ mosquitoes, specifically ‘Aedes aegypti’ and ‘Aedes notoscriptus,’ have been invading coastal areas of Southern California. These mosquitoes are capable of spreading dengue fever and are known for biting around the feet and ankles during the day.
Scale, impact and risk
Santa Barbara County’s Mosquito and Vector Management District confirmed the presence of four ‘Aedes aegypti’ mosquitoes. The article also notes that West Nile virus cases in Los Angeles County have spiked to over 180 this year, and Orange County has pulled 850 traps with West Nile-positive bugs. However, no confirmed dengue cases or deaths are reported in the supplied evidence.
Response, status and uncertainty
Residents are advised to contact the District if they encounter aggressive mosquitoes indoors or outdoors during the day. The agency is conducting mosquito trapping and monitoring activities. The article does not provide details on the current status of the investigation or any confirmed risk assessments related to dengue virus transmission.

海南三亚工地爆登革热疫情 数千工人被隔离

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

海南省三亚市多个高端住宅工地近日爆发登革热疫情,数千名建筑工人随后被封控隔离,多名工人向大纪元透露,工地疑已有数十至数百人感染,全员被多次抽血检测。部分工人被转移至当地酒店或居民小区,引发事前不知情的小区业主不满。时值中共二十届五中全会在北京召开,敏感的时间节点使防控全面升级。据悉,三亚市市长亲赴现场督导,警方24小时严密把守入口,严禁人员随意进出,防控措施极为严苛。
